[Table_Title] 
假期扰动 PMI走势，内外需双双放缓

[Table_ReportType] 
——10月PMI数据点评

[Table_ReportDate] 2026年11月02日

[Table_Summary] ⚫ 事件： 10月中采制造业PMI为 49.3%，较上月下跌0.8个
百分点，非制造业PMI由50.2%下跌至49.7%。 
⚫ 制造业PMI环比季节性回落，中小企业景气跌幅较大。PMI
再度降至临界值下方，工作天数减少带来一定影响。中、小
型企业PMI回落幅度较大，均处于临近值下方，大型企业
PMI小幅回落，仍处于景气区间。 
⚫ 产需双双回落，进出口景气度降温，价格指标景气度回落。
生产指数降至临界值下方，新订单指数下跌，生产回落快于
需求。新出口订单、进口指数下跌，而欧美日等制造业PMI
景气度整体保持旺盛。企业采购意愿下滑，生产放缓影响企
业采购需求。纺织、有色金属、非金属矿等行业生产、新订
单偏弱。原材料价格回落幅度大于出厂价格跌幅，中下游企
业成本压力边际缓解，有色金属板块价格跌幅较大，能耗产
业链价格维持高景气。原材料库存累积、产成品库存去化的
趋势持续，主动补库存周期尚不稳固。 
⚫ 高技术、装备制造高位放缓，消费品、基础原材料、钢铁行
业景气度下滑。新经济景气度高位回落，但仍处景气区间，
EPMI大幅拉升，供需持续好转，秋季旺季特征明显，采购
价格持续上行，但速度放缓。SMI中枢上行，中上游制造业
显著修复。消费品行业PMI重回荣枯线下方，双节备货拉动
消退后，内需接续效应不足。兰格钢铁PMI下滑幅度较大，
销售价格和采购成本大幅回落，供需均转弱。 
⚫ 建筑业为非制造业PMI主要拖累，服务业PMI处于临界点。
非制造业PMI新订单和新出口订单指数下滑幅度较大，需求
持续偏弱，价格指标回落。其中，建筑业PMI下滑至临界值
下方，工作日减少带来一定影响，新订单指数跌幅较大，建
筑业销售价格上行，投入品价格回落，钢材价格下跌形成一
定拖累。服务业PMI小幅放缓，新订单指数跌幅较大，价格
指数回落，仍处景气区间，十一长假给价格带来支撑。 
⚫ 季节性因素干扰PMI表现，政策扶持下四季度经济仍有支
撑。10月受长假和外部扰动等影响，PMI环比承压，内外需
均放缓。BCI企业利润、销售前瞻指数小幅回落，销售前瞻
指数维持在60以上，仍具韧性，油价上行给利润带来一定
压力，融资环境指数小幅上行。随着财政资金逐步落地，有
望加快形成实物工作量，带动基建、制造业投资好转。 
⚫ 风险因素：地缘政治风险发展超预期，政策出台和落地不及
预期，外需回落幅度超预期。

采矿业为利润主要拉动，企业库存持续累积 
财政支出有望继续提速

万联证券研究所 www.wlzq.cn 第 3 页 共 7 页  
事件 
2026年10月，中采制造业PMI为49.3%， 较上月下跌0.8个百分点， 非制造业PMI为49.7%，
较上月下跌0.5个百分点，综合PMI为49.7%，环比下跌1个百分点。

1    PMI环比季节性回落，产需双双放缓 
制造业PMI环比季节性回落，中小企业景气回落幅度较大。制造业PMI环比回落0.8个
百分点至49.3%，再度降至临界值下方，工作天数减少带来一定影响，财政投放提速，
项目建设给PMI表现形成支撑。从不同规模企业来看，小型企业PMI下行1.4个百分点
至47.5%， 中型企业PMI下行1.3个百分点至48.4%， 大型企业PMI由50.6%回落至50.2%，
仅大型企业处于景气区间，中小企业依然承压。 
高技术、装备制造高位继续回落，战略新兴产业显著回暖，消费品、基础原材料、钢
铁行业景气度下滑。高技术制造业继续下行1.2个百分点至51.3%，装备制造业由51%
回落为50.6%，高端制造业景气度持续回落，但仍处景气区间。基础原材料行业由48%
回落为47.3%。消费品行业下行1.9个百分点至48.8%，重回荣枯线下方，双节备货拉
动消退后，内需接续效应不足。战略性新兴产业PMI（EPMI）由52.3%拉升至58%，达
到年内高点，其中，生产量、产品订货和进口指数上行幅度较大，供需双双好转，秋
季旺季特征明显， 但进口指数仍处荣枯线下方， 采购价格持续上行， 但速度明显放缓，
就业和研发指标也有所上行。光伏经理人指数（SMI）10月中枢较9月上行，中上游制
造业显著修复，下游电站小幅回落。兰格钢铁PMI由49.8%回落为46.9%，下滑幅度较
大，其中，销售价格和采购成本大幅回落，下行至荣枯线下方，销量、总订单、国内
订单也明显回落，出口订单量小幅下滑，仅库存水平环比上行。

图表1：10月制造业PMI环比季节性回落（%） 图表2：高技术制造、装备制造业PMI继续回落（%）

资料来源：Wind，万联证券研究所 资料来源：Wind，万联证券研究所

口指数环比转为下跌2.2个百分点至47%。采购量下行2.1个百分点至48.9%，生产放缓
影响企业采购需求回落。供货商配送时间由50.1%上行至50.2%。从统计局公布数据来
看，铁路船舶航空航天设备、计算机通信电子设备等行业生产指数和新订单指数均位
于53.0%及以上；纺织、非金属矿物制品、有色金属冶炼及压延加工等行业生产指数
和新订单指数均低于临界点，地产持续偏弱对上游的拖累作用持续，部分有色金属价
格、库存偏高给产需带来一定影响。美国制造业PMI高位边际放缓，但景气度仍强，
日本制造业PMI持续小幅回落，仍处荣枯线上方。德国、英国制造业PMI上行，处于临
界点上方，法国受国内动乱影响，制造业PMI降至荣枯线下方。 
图表3：供需分项环比回落，价格指标下行（%） 图表4：美国制造业PMI小幅回落，景气仍强（%）

资料来源：Wind，万联证券研究所 资料来源：Wind，万联证券研究所 
价格指标景气度回落，原材料和加工行业剪刀差收窄，有色行业价格下滑为主要拖
累，能化产业链维持高景气。主要原材料购进价格回落4个百分点至56.8%，出厂价格
下行2.3个百分点至51.7%，原材料价格回落幅度大于出厂价格跌幅，中下游企业成本
压力边际缓解。10月，受到宏观流动性收紧预期和基本面供需矛盾等因素影响，有色
金属板块承压，金、银、钴、碳酸锂等有色金属跌幅较大。国际油价持续攀升，石油
煤炭及其他燃料加工、化学纤维及橡胶塑料制品等行业价格指数维持高景气。 
原材料库存累积、产成品库存去化的趋势持续，主动补库存周期尚不稳固。产成品库
存由47.6%下跌至47.5%， 原材料库存上行0.2个百分点至48.4%。原料端价格压力持续，
企业备库需求和价格增长带动原材料库存上行。生产经营活动预期指标由53.8%回落
为53.4%。 
图表5：原材料库存累积，产成品库存去化（%） 图表6：原材料价格和出厂价格价差明显收窄（%）

2    非制造业PMI回落，建筑业为主要拖累 
非制造业PMI回落，需求持续偏弱，价格指标回落。10月非制造业PMI为49.7%，较上
月下跌0.5个百分点。其中，新订单和新出口订单指数下滑幅度较大，持续处于临近
值下方，在手订单指数也有所回落。商务活动指数下滑，降至临近值下方。投入品价
格回落幅度大于销售价格回落幅度。业务活动预期由55.5%回落至54.7%，企业经营预
期下跌，但仍处景气区间。仅从业人员由45.9%小幅上行至46%。 
建筑业PMI下滑至临界值下方，新订单指数下滑幅度较大，工作日减少带来一定影响，
销售价格和投入品价格走势分化。建筑业PMI下跌至48%，上月为50.3%。其中，建筑
业新订单指数下跌5.7个百分点至40%， 建筑业业务活动预期下跌3.1个百分点至52.3%，
建筑业销售价格上行，投入品价格回落，钢材价格下跌形成一定拖累。随着财政资金
落地， “六张网”建设逐步显效，有望带动建筑业回暖。 
服务业PMI处于临界点，新订单指数持续偏弱，价格指数回落。服务业PMI为50%，较
上月下跌0.2个百分点。服务业新订单指数下跌，回落幅度相对最大。服务业投入品
和销售价格均下跌，仍处景气区间，十一长假给价格带来支撑。业务活动预期指数小
幅回落，仍处临界值上方。从统计局公布数据来看，电信广播电视及卫星传输服务、
货币金融服务、保险等行业商务活动指数持续位于55.0%以上的高景气区间；资本市
场服务、房地产等行业商务活动指数低位运行。

图表7：建筑业PMI新订单跌幅较大（%） 图表8：服务业新订单下跌(%)

资料来源：Wind，万联证券研究所 资料来源：Wind，万联证券研究所

制造业用工需求持平，服务业就业好转，建筑业就业回落。10月制造业PMI从业人员
持平于上月的48.4%。服务业从业人员较上月上行0.5个百分点至46.7%，建筑业从业
人员指数为42%，较上月下跌2.3个百分点，十一长假影响建筑业用工需求，给服务业
就业带来提振。

3    总结与展望 
季节性因素干扰PMI表现，政策扶持下四季度经济仍有支撑。10月受长假和外部扰动
等影响，PMI环比承压，内外需均放缓。BCI企业利润、销售前瞻指数小幅回落，销售
前瞻指数维持在60以上，仍具韧性，油价上行给利润带来一定压力，融资环境指数小
幅上行。随着财政资金逐步落地，有望加快形成实物工作量，带动基建、制造业投资
好转。

图表9：企业利润、销售前瞻指数小幅回落（%） 图表10：企业融资环境指数小幅上行（%）

资料来源：Wind，万联证券研究所 资料来源：Wind，万联证券研究所

4    风险提示 
地缘政治风险发展超预期，政策出台和落地不及预期，外需回落幅度超预期。

强于大市：未来6个月内行业指数相对大盘涨幅10％以上； 
同步大市：未来6个月内行业指数相对大盘涨幅10％至-10％之间； 
弱于大市：未来6个月内行业指数相对大盘跌幅10％以上。

[Table_StockRankInfo] 公司投资评级 
买入：未来6个月内公司相对大盘涨幅15％以上； 
增持：未来6个月内公司相对大盘涨幅5％至15％； 
观望：未来6个月内公司相对大盘涨幅-5％至5％； 
卖出：未来6个月内公司相对大盘跌幅5％以上。 
基准指数 ：沪深300指数

[Table_RiskInfo] 风险提示 
我们在此提醒您，不同证券研究机构采用不同的评级术语及评级标准。我们采用的是相对评级体系，表示投资的
相对比重建议；投资者买入或者卖出证券的决定取决于个人的实际情况，比如当前的持仓结构以及其他需要考
虑的因素。投资者应阅读整篇报告，以获取比较完整的观点与信息，不应仅仅依靠投资评级来推断结论。

出具本报告。本报告清晰准确地反映了本人的研究观点。本人不曾因，不因，也将不会因本报告中的具体推荐意
见或观点而直接或间接收到任何形式的补偿。

事件描述：
 两场座谈会同步落地：央行、金监总局、证监会于 11 月 2 日先后召开
“证券、基金、保险公司互换便利座谈会”和“股票回购增持再贷款座
谈会”。证监会副主席刘浩凌、央行副行长邹澜、金监总局副局长丛林
出席，23 家证券基金公司、26 家银行参会。
 两项工具“从应急走向制度化”：（1）互换便利（SFISF）：余额 1050
亿元，第三批扩容至 31 家券商/基金公司，申请额度超 1000 亿元，11
月 3 日第三次 SFISF 操作实际落地 1000 亿元，31 家机构参与，中标费
率 10bp，期限 1 年可展期，后续将“适当增加参与机构、降低互换费率、
优化质押模式”。（2）回购增持再贷款：签约约 4400 亿、累计发放超
2800 亿；由阶段性安排转为长期制度性安排，再贷款利率从 1.25%降至

1%。
 证监会答记者问的四条政策线：（1）工具端：引导权重指数公司、龙头
科技股做出标志性回购案例，督促已披露计划加速实施。（2）产品端：
18 只主动 ETF 募资超 350 亿（16 只触达 20 亿上限），加快建仓；继续
推权益类 ETF、股债恒定比例多资产指数基金、浮动费率基金。（3）两
融端：研究优化融资保证金比例、折算率、维持担保比例，适度释放融
资空间，盘活存量流动性。（4）回购规则：要求年度分红划出比例纳入
回购专项基金、超募资金强制部分回购、护盘式回购收益闭环用于注销。
投资要点：定价机制改变，而非资金本身。政策通过降低回购成本、提供机
构流动性支持、优化杠杆管理参数三条路径，系统性降低了权益资产的持有
成本和交易摩擦。政策影响的量化特征呈现“小资金、大乘数”结构，三项
工具可动用资金规模占自由流通市值仅约 1.42%，但通过成本压降、EPS 增
厚、PE 扩张三条乘数路径，潜在影响规模可达数万亿级别，同时两融松绑
作为弹性增强器可改善存量资金的使用效率，提升短线风险偏好。

 规模维度：政策工具属催化剂而非主要增量资金。政策工具来看，互换
便利余额 2050 亿元，占 A 股自由流通市值约 0.43%；回购增持再贷款
签约约 4400 亿，占 A 股自由流通市值约 0.92%，其中累计发放超 2800
亿，占 A 股自由流通市值约 0.58%；主动 ETF 募资 350 亿元，占 A 股
自由流通市值约 0.07%。三项工具可动用规模合计约 6,800 亿元，占全
A 流通市值约 1.42%。量级上可见政策工具并非可直接托举指数的体量，
而是通过压低风险溢价和交易成本来撬动市场自身定价，属于催化剂而
非主要增量资金投放。
 成本维度：再贷款降息的财务影响。再贷款利率从 1.25%降至 1%，存
量 2800 亿对应年利息成本从 35 亿元降至 28 亿元，年省 7 亿元，降幅
20%；若增量 1600 亿（签约 4400 亿元−已发 2800 亿元）全部按 1%投

### Page 2

放，年成本仅 16 亿元。4400 亿元全量按 1%，年总成本 44 亿元，比旧
利率省 11 亿元/年。对单家回购公司，成本下降直接转化为回购的净收
益，1%的利率已低于多数高分红公司的股息率，回购注销的套利空间
从接近中性变为明确正收益。
 盈利维度：回购注销对 EPS 的增厚。用公式 EPS 增厚 ≈ 回购额/市值
÷ (1−回购额/市值) 测算，假设温和情景回购占市值比为 0.6%（略高于
2800 亿元占全 A 市值比）、中性情景回购占市值比为 1%、积极情景回
购占市值比为 1.4%，EPS 增厚分别约 0.60%、1.01%、1.42%，回购额
占全 A 归母总净利（假设 Q3 线推）比分别约为 8.78%、14.63%、20.48%，
回购额整体不超过年利润，并未构成对存量现金的消耗，高 ROE 和强
现金流的公司为这一机制的受益者。
 杠杆维度：两融保证金比例调整的弹性。从保证金比例、折算率、维持
担保比例三个维度同时研究优化，表明监管层有意释放存量融资空间的
效率。融资保证金比例下调后，存量保证金可撬动千亿资金。以 2026

年 10 月 30 日实际融资余额 23078.12 亿元为基准，按监管通用公式推导，
若保证金比例从当前的 100%下调至新比例 95%、90%、80%，理论增
量分别可达 1214.64 亿元、2564.24 亿元、4072.61 亿元，考虑参与率、
风险偏好、集中度等因素，实际增量亦可达百亿元，潜在杠杆资金是短
期弹性的主要来源，也是波动放大的潜在根源。
 机构维度：互换便利的扩容效应。叠加存量展期余额 1050 亿元，全市
场可参考的互换便利支持池约 2050 亿量级。31 家机构分享 1000 亿新增
申请，均值约 32.3 亿元/家，若全额操作，占全 A 日均成交额（假设积
极情景约 1.8 万亿）的 5.56%，10bp 互换费率下，1000 亿年互换费用仅
约 1 亿元，利润影响可忽略，信号意义远大于成本意义，工具已完成从
应急到常态化的定位切换。单家 32.3 亿元的流动性额度，对头部券商而
言是“低成本流动性保险”， 对中型机构是打破融资分层的关键通道。
机构维度的价值不在利润弹性，而在降低系统性被动抛售概率。

 估值维度：盈利底与政策底的乘数效应。政策工具通过压缩流动性折价、
治理不确定折价、消除尾部风险预期压缩分母端风险溢价，撬动整个市
场估值中枢上移的乘数效应。全 A 总市值 116.45 万亿，三季报全 A 归
母净利润 5.10 万亿，隐含 PE 14.63 倍，PE 每扩张 1 倍，对应市值增量
7.96 万亿，相当于当前总市值的 6.83%。这是最关键的量化锚点：政策
工具压缩的是风险溢价，直接体现为 PE 扩张，14.63 倍的隐含 PE 为这
一扩张提供了估值空间，而 7.96 万亿的每倍 PE 弹性说明政策的边际影
响在估值端远大于在资金端。
风险提示：政策执行不及预期；盈利下修对冲估值扩张的风险；海外流动性
收紧与外资风险偏好外溢；两融杠杆双向放大与结构性脆弱；回购注销执行
质量与制度套利风险；盈利预期与政策工具落地节奏潜在乘数效应影响等。

市場焦點
美國
• 9月CPI年增3.7%、PPI年增6.3%，雙雙高於前期 ，能源價格的急升是通膨回升的主要推力 。
雖然核心CPI年增率僅升至2.6%，但住房與服務價格仍維持黏著，使通膨降溫過程出現反覆；
同時PPI增幅明顯高於 CPI，反映企業成本壓力偏高 ，未來不排除進一步向終端價格傳導 。整
體而言，CPI與PPI同步回升反映通膨降溫進程仍存在不確定性 ，進而令市場重估通膨回落速
度與對Fed貨幣政策路徑的審慎預期。
AI
• AI雲端業者 CoreWeave的5年期CDS利差一度突破 1,140個基點 ，旗下兩檔債券殖利率逼近
15%，相關賣壓並進一步蔓延至股票市場 。全球最大半導體設備商ASML表示，客戶已開始洽
談2029至2030年的訂單；晶圓代工龍頭台積電財報亦優於市場預期 。與此同時，台灣出口增

速創3月以來新高，韓國9月半導體出口年增逾260%，顯示半導體需求持續強勁，預期第三季
相關企業獲利仍有望維持強勁。
中國
• 9月通膨加速主要源於中東能源衝擊及 AI硬體需求，能源和科技領域之外 ，普遍性的通縮壓力
仍在，彭博表示以GDP平減指數衡量 ，經歷三年下滑後 ，整體經濟預計將在 2026年走出通縮 ，
投資上短期可聚焦，受惠AI資本支出與能源價格上行的半導體、伺服器及能源相關族群。

美股獲利引擎轉向半導體/核心AI與能源
• 雖然標普500指數在2026年第三季展現出獲利韌性 ，但隨著油價上漲侵蝕利潤率 、消費者受通膨壓抑 ，以及分析師下調未
來預測動能，市場未來的容錯空間正在縮小。
• AI與半導體仍為獲利領頭羊：科技與半導體持續為標普500指數獲利成長的核心動力，其中約20家企業組成的核心AI一籃子
股票(權重約34.6%)對指數獲利的貢獻度已超過科技七雄(Mag 7)，且此優勢預計將至少延續至2028年底。
• 油價推升能源產業，接棒成為強棒：受到近期國際油價上揚影響，能源產業獲利預測顯著上修，預計獲利成長勁道將延續至
2027年初。在接下來的三個季度中，能源板塊的收益成長表現預計將超越科技七雄。
• 消費與運輸業面臨成本壓頂：相較於科技與能源的強勁，必需消費品與交通運輸業正承受顯著壓力。例如，達美航空受高燃

料成本拖累，而Nike與百事可樂則反映出消費者因持續通膨而縮減支出的困境。
7月以來EPS和營收成長預期修正

顧
利潤率高檔盤整、關稅退款帶來短補，但動能已見放緩
• 利潤率高檔盤整，淨利潤率提升受限：標普500成分股第二季營運利潤率(18.7%)與淨利潤率(15.6%)均創下新高。雖然第三
季營運利潤率預估可進一步提升至19.4%，但淨利潤率預計微幅上升至15.8%，顯示整體企業淨利潤的擴張動能正漸趨平緩 。
• 700億美元關稅退款提供短線補藥： 受惠於美國政府依據國際緊急經濟權力法 (IEEPA)的追償退稅，估計仍有至少700億美元
的關稅款將退還給標普 500成分股(主要集中於工業、非必需消費及醫療保健等 )，這預計能為2026年標普500指數每股收益
(EPS)成長帶來約235個基點、利潤率提升約34個基點的短期提振。
• 上修動能開始轉弱：雖然整體財報指引動量仍維持正值，但過去六週內，分析師對未來12個月EPS與收入的修正動能出現頻

繁下修，這意味著市場先前的極度樂觀情緒正在消退，未來財報季度要優於預期的門檻正在提高。
• 小結與投資建議： 標普500指數短期內靠著半導體 /AI與能源雙引擎拉抬 ，加上關稅退款的一次性利多注入 ，下半年整體獲
利依然具備支撐。然而，高油價帶來的營運成本上升 ，以及終端消費力道的疲軟 ，正逐漸侵蝕非科技/非能源企業的利潤 ，
而大盤廣度不足(僅少數板塊支撐獲利)，代表未來投資風險正逐漸集中 ，但AI算力需求仍屬硬性需求，但波動加劇下，投資
建議以定期定額方式投資美股核心AI與半導體龍頭股票。

美國9月CPI、PPI 同步升溫，能源衝擊擴大通膨與利率風險
• 能源價格急升，消費通膨再升溫：美國9月CPI月增0.6%、
年增3.7%，高於前月 3.4%，其中汽油月增 9.1%、能源
價格月增 5.2%，汽油單項即貢獻整體 CPI當月漲幅逾半；
另一方面，核心CPI月增0.3%、年增2.6%、住房指數月
增0.4%，顯示能源雖是本次通膨回升主因 ，但住房及部
分服務價格仍具黏著性，令通膨降溫進程面臨反覆。
• 上游價格大幅攀升 ，成本傳導風險升高： 9月PPI最終需
求月增1.3%、年增6.3%，創2022年3月以來最大增幅，
其中最終需求商品月增 2.6%，能源價格大漲 10.8%，汽
油價格更跳升 20.3%；核心PPI月增0.5%、年增5.0%，
顯示價格壓力不僅來自能源 ，也涉及部分商品與服務環

節；後續是否進一步推升至 CPI，仍須觀察企業成本轉
嫁程度及需求變化。
• 市場看法： 通膨回升恐提高 Fed再度升息機率 ，並延長
高利率維持時間 ，甚至不排除進一步緊縮的可能 ，短期
有利支撐美債殖利率及美元表現 ，不過股、債市後續走
勢仍將受到貨幣政策預期與風險情緒變化影響 。資產配
置建議可關注具成本轉嫁能力 、現金流穩健的能源類股；
債券則宜以短中天期 、高信用評等標的為主 ，以降低利
率敏感度。黃金可作為分散地緣政治及通膨風險的輔助
配置，但不宜將任何單一資產視為必然受惠標的。

供給面推動中國9月CPI升0.9%
• 供給面推動中國9月通膨復甦：中國9月通膨數據顯示，CPI年增率由8月的0.8%升至0.9%，連續兩個月擴大，但低於市場預期
的1.0%，物價上漲主要受到能源及旅遊價格帶動，而食品價格跌幅進一步擴大，使整體通膨壓力仍相對溫和，扣除食品與能源
後，核心CPI維持在1.0%，反映內需需求尚未明顯升溫 。另一方面，PPI年增率由3.8%大幅升至5.0%，優於市場預期，且月增
率達1.2%，創4月以來最大漲幅，顯示上游原物料及工業品價格回升，須關注未來是否逐步傳導至消費端價格。
• 展望後市： 9月通膨加速主要源於中東能源衝擊及 AI硬體需求 ，能源和科技領域之外 ，普遍性的通縮壓力仍 在，彭博表示 以
GDP平減指數衡量，經歷三年下滑後，整體經濟預計將在2026年走出通縮，值得注意的是，這種供給面驅動的通膨復甦，將降

低官方推出需求刺激措施的緊迫感 ，而要反轉通縮循環正需要官方舉措，投資上短期可聚焦受惠AI資本支出與能源價格上行的
半導體、伺服器及能源相關族群。
資料來源：彭博，2026.10.14
通膨集中於能源和AI相關行業

過去18個月來，新興市場股票表現亮眼，累積報酬率超過標普500指數
的兩倍
• 過去18個月來，新興市場股票表現亮眼，累積報酬率超過標普500指數的兩倍：主因為獲利帶動成長，整體而言，新興市場企
業獲利表現強勁 ，而且仍持續加速成長 ，高盛預估2026年新興市場企業盈餘成長率將達 55%，超過標普500企業盈餘成長速
度的兩倍，且新興市場股票估值較美國股市存在超過40%的折價空間，評價相對具吸引力，今年以來流入新興市場股市的資金
已接近500億美元，創下過去20年來最強勁紀錄之一。更重要的是新興市場國家目前貢獻了全球近 80%的AI硬體出口。隨著AI
產業持續發展，受惠範圍有望從少數龍頭企業擴大至更多國家及公司，特別是那些能夠協助解決供應鏈瓶頸的企業。
• 結論：後市來看在AI產業趨勢、相對低估的評價水準，以及強勁企業獲利成長三大因素支撐下，新興市場股票未來一年仍具備

進一步上漲的潛力，有機會延續目前的強勢表現。

AI投資支撐美國成長，融資需求升高使長端利率與信用風險並存
• AI基礎建設持續擴張 ，成為企業投資重要動能 ：大型雲端業者
持續投入資料中心 、半導體、網路設備與電力基礎設施 ，帶動
相關產業資本支出與供應鏈需求 。摩根大通資產管理指出 ，納
入資料中心開發商及半導體投資後，AI 基礎建設至 2030 年的
累計投資需求可能達約 5兆美元 ，顯示AI投資循環仍具規模 ，
但實際經濟貢獻與企業獲利 ，仍取決於投資效率及商業化成果 。
• 外部融資需求增加 ，債券供給與投資回報成關鍵： 隨AI投資規
模擴大，大型科技企業逐步增加公司債與外部融資 ，在企業債
與公債供給同步增加下 ，長天期利率面臨上行壓力 。若未來AI
獲利成長未能跟上資本支出擴張速度 ，可能壓縮自由現金流與

資本報酬率，進一步擴大信用利差 ，並提高高估值科技股的評
價修正風險
• 市場觀察：AI基礎建設仍是未來數年推動全球科技投資與企業
數位轉型的成長主軸，相關需求具備中長期發展空間，而2026
年市場已逐漸轉向檢視投資回報率 、現金流創造能力及融資結
構是否具備可持續性 ，因此單純依賴資本支出擴張已不足以支
撐所有AI概念股評價 。建議配置可兼顧具獲利與現金流支撐的
AI供應鏈，以及高信用評等 、中短天期債券；對長天期債券則
須評估供給與利率風險 ，並審慎檢視高負債 AI企業的償債能力
及投資回報。
資料來源：Goldman Sachs，永豐金證券， 2026.10.12

AI熱潮、高債務與網路威脅，IMF示警三大金融穩定風險升高
資料來源：Bloomberg Finance L.P， Dealogic，Zero day Clock and IMF staff calculations.， 2026.10.13
AI相關風險持續升溫 主權債券殖利率攀升 網路資安威脅升高
大型雲端科技企業債券發行規模
（占投資等級公司債市場比重) 10年期公債殖利率平均變動幅度 零時差漏洞占比（%）
• AI估值高度集中，市場重定價風險升溫：IMF於10月《全球金融穩定報告》指出，AI相關投資已成為全球成長的重要支撐，
但資金與漲勢高度集中於少數企業及市場，一旦獲利成長不如預期或估值遭重新檢視，可能引發科技股修正並透過風險偏好
下降擴散至整體金融市場。
• 通膨與財政疑慮恐推升債券市場波動： IMF認為，若能源價格推升通膨壓力或市場對財政永續性產生疑慮 ，主權債殖利率可

能進一步上升，進而提高全球融資成本與資產重估風險；在高利率與高債務環境下，金融條件快速緊縮的情況將明顯提高。
• 網路安全風險成為潛在威脅： 隨金融體系數位化程度提高 ，IMF將大型網路攻擊列為重要風險來源之一 。若攻擊事件波及支
付系統、金融基礎設施或雲端服務平台，不僅可能影響市場運作效率，也可能透過信心衝擊與流動性壓力擴大金融市場波動。
• 投資建議：市場成長動能仍受AI支撐，但在金融穩定風險上升情境下，建議採取「AI受惠股＋高品質債券＋防禦型資產」的
槓鈴策略；核心配置可聚焦全球AI基礎建設供應鏈及投資等級債，藉由企業長期獲利成長與債券避險功能，同時因應估值修
正及金融條件收緊風險。

AI隱憂浮現但動能仍在
• CoreWeave信用風險亮紅燈：市場憂心雲端業者在算力租賃業務產生足夠回報前，恐難以負擔龐大債務，導致信用風險升
溫。上週，AI雲端業者CoreWeave的5年期信用違約交換（CDS）利差一度突破1,140個基點，升至通常反映嚴重信用壓力
的水準；旗下兩檔債券殖利率逼近15%，其中2032年到期的無擔保債券於上週三跌破面值的80%，相關賣壓並進一步蔓延
至股票市場。
• AI半導體產業的基本面仍具韌性：全球最大半導體設備商艾司摩爾（ASML）表示，客戶已開始洽談2029至2030年的訂單；
晶圓代工龍頭台積電財報亦優於市場預期。與此同時，台灣出口增速創3月以來新高，韓國9月半導體出口年增逾260%，顯
示半導體需求持續強勁，預期第三季相關企業獲利仍有望維持強勁。

美銀美林調查指出，全球經理人對景氣與股市仍維持偏多立場
資料來源：BofA Global Fund Manager Survey，2026.10
• 經理人樂觀情緒維持高檔： 美銀美林10月全球基金經理人調查顯示 ，市場風險偏好仍高 ，平均現金水位維持在 3.9%的低檔，
科技股配置則升至2020年8月以來最高，反映經理人對AI產業及企業獲利前景仍具信心 。然而，整體經濟成長預期已轉趨保
守，淨4%的受訪者預期未來 12個月經濟成長放緩 。極端風險方面，債券殖利率無序攀升仍居首位 ，占比31%；AI泡沫及通
膨再起分別為23%及20%。此外，39%的經理人認為，大型雲端業者資本支出是系統性信用事件的主要潛在來源 ，顯示市場
焦點已從AI需求成長延伸至龐大資本支出的獲利回收能力。

• 美國公債殖利率走高 ，債券的收益吸引力逐漸回升： 調查顯示，37%的受訪者表示，當美國10年期公債殖利率升至 6%時，
才會考慮由股票轉向債券；另有19%認為目前殖利率已具吸引力，顯示部分經理人已開始重新評估固定收益資產的配置價值 。
不過，債券整體仍處淨減碼，且經理人對科技股的配置持續升高 ，反映市場尚未全面轉向防禦。後續仍須關注美債殖利率變
化、AI資本支出的投資回報，以及企業獲利能否支撐偏高的市場評價。
資料來源：BofA Global Fund Manager Survey，2026.10
基金經理人現金水位 最可能影響市場表現的極端風險

台積電財報佳卻未激勵股價，短線面臨總經干擾
• 台積電Q3營收、獲利及EPS同步改寫單季新高：營收年增50.9%、季增17.6%，稅後純益7,630.6億元，年增68.7％、季增8
％；每股稅後純益(EPS)29.43元，整體優於市場預期，但毛利率66.4％較第二季的67.7％下降。
• 財測展望：第四季財測營收預估 523~543億美元，季增上看15％、年增65％，毛利率預估65%~67%，反映高階晶片需求
依然強勁。全年展望方面，預期2026年美元營收年增約44%，較上季預估的「略高於40%」說法更加明確。此外，重申全
年資本支出600~640億美元不變。
• 市場反應：台積電法說會隔日股價小幅下跌，法說會未出現激勵行情，主要原因包含美國通膨升溫壓力、外資連續調節部位，

以及毛利率略為下降。短線籌碼面可能仍會調整，但台積電董事長魏哲家在法說會中強調，第二波AI需求正強勁興起，台積
電正透過多種途徑全力擴充產能，顯示高階晶片前景明確，中長期具投資價值，多家投資機構也紛紛上調目標價。
資料來源：台積電法說會，兆豐銀行整理，2026.10.15。
法說會重點整理 說明
AI需求持續升溫
魏哲家坦言，目前仍難以判斷晶圓供需缺口將擴大或縮小，因為
客戶需求持續增加，台積電也正積極提高產能。儘管消費性電子
市場仍受到零組件漲價及總體經濟不確定性影響，但先進製程需
求依然強勁，公司預估2026年全年美元營收將年增約44％。
擴廠進度
(1)台南科學園區新建3奈米廠預計2027年下半年量產，並決定在
台南再增建一座3奈米廠，規畫2028年投入量產。
(2)美國亞利桑那州第二座晶圓廠已完成廠房興建，預計2027年
第三季量產，時程較原先規畫略為提前；日本第二座晶圓廠則將
導入3奈米製程，預計2028年量產。

均良好，尚未發現明顯問題。

錯過漲幅

• 全球經濟正處於「韌性仍在、風險升高」的階段：國際貨幣基金組織(IMF)指出，AI投資熱潮持續推動美國、台灣等國家成
長，但中東地緣政治衝突、高通膨風險、政府債務問題與AI泡沫化疑慮，也同時為市場埋下不確定因素。
• 市場永遠充滿雜音，定期定額紀律投資更具優勢：本許多人認為定期定額的核心在於「平均成本」，但更重要的是幫助投資
人克服人性。市場上漲時持續投入，可以參與企業獲利成長帶來的成果，市場下跌時持續扣款，則能以較低價格累積更多單
位數。換句話說，定期定額讓投資人不需要每天判斷：明天會不會跌？現在是不是太貴？要不要先停扣觀望？長期而言，紀

律投資往往比短期擇時的成效更佳。
• AI時代更適合定期定額：儘管IMF認為人工智慧發展為全球經濟成長帶來貢獻，但同時也警告，若市場對AI未來獲利能力過
度樂觀，不排除出現股市估值修正。根據歷史經驗，每一次市場重大修正往往也孕育下一輪成長契機，不論AI發展是否出現
泡沫，善用定期定額策略可以在機會與風險之間達到平衡。
韌性仍在、風險升溫，紀律投資更穩健
任一月份定期定額進場投資試算

息日前一日之淨值×100%。

2  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/
上週重要市場資訊
⚫ 澳洲央行決議如預期升息一碼至 4.60%為今年第四度升息，基準利率創 2011 年
以來新高，澳洲央行在政策聲明中表示通膨壓力尚未緩解，不排除在11、12 月
政策會議進一步升息；另一方面，澳洲8 月消費者物價指數(CPI)月增 0.4%略低
於預期，但年增 4.0%仍高於澳洲央行 2~3%的目標區間。
⚫ 美國第二季美 GDP 終值從 1.5%上修至 2.2%， 其中實質個人消費支出創下2025
年 3 月以來最大單月增幅，顯示即使高利率持續，美國家庭消費依然具韌性；
另一方面，美國 8 月核心 PCE 物價指數年增月增 0.2%、3.0%，雙雙低於市場預
期的 0.3%、3.3%，美國 9 月非農就業人數新增 2.9 萬人低於預期的 9 萬人，失
業率由 4.1%上升至 4.2%，8 月非農就業人數亦同步下修至 13.3 萬人，通膨下滑
和勞動力市場略有降溫，降低市場對聯準會 10 月升息預期，Fedwatch 顯示 10

月升息機率下降至 20%左右。
⚫ 受美伊衝突引發的能源價格衝擊影響 ，9 月歐元地區消費者物價調和指數(HICP)
整體上升速度遠超預期，其中，法國年增 3.4%、德國年增 3.3%，歐元區年增
3.8%，均高於歐洲央行的 2%目標，反映能源價格的衝擊持續影響物價，加深歐
洲央行再次升息的壓力。
⚫ 日本上週公佈貨幣政策會議紀要及央行意見摘要顯示多名委員認為當前政策利
率依然處於中性區間之下 ，需警惕通膨上行風險 ， 且未來應該以更快速度升息抑
制通膨，然而意見摘要提及日本經濟財政大臣城內實表示希望央行充分審視過
去升息對景氣之影響 ，市場解讀為政府牽制日本央行未來升息步伐 ， 而財務大臣
片山皋月則重申尊重日本央行的自主性。

### Page 3

3  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
 美元兌新台幣匯率走勢  
Oct/5/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
上週台幣匯價高點在 31.760，低點在 31.946，以 31.830 作收，較前一週收盤價
貶值 5 分。 
上週台幣震盪走貶，週初，國際油價持穩於每桶 100 美元上方，市場預期聯準
會後續仍有升息的空間，提振美元指數表現，加以外資賣超台股並匯出，台幣走貶
至 31.900 上方；不過適逢月底也是季底，匯價逢高吸引出口商進場賣美金，緩解台
幣貶值的速度；臨近週末，美國 8 月核心 PCE 數據 3%低於預期 3.3%，但市場等待
9 月非農就業數據，交投偏向謹慎觀望，最終在央行調節之下收在 31.830。

＜未來分析＞ 
    預估本週台幣區間震盪。就國際市場而言，美國 9 月非農就業人數新增 2.9 萬
人，低於市場預期 9 萬人，且失業率由 4.1%上升至 4.2%，顯示就業市場動能略有
減弱，市場對於 10 月升息的預期下降，但仍預期 12 月會再度升息。美元指數雖然
脫離高點，但仍在 102 附近高位震盪，預計將給台幣帶來壓力；就台灣市場來看，
台股再創收盤新高且外資轉為買超台股，且隨著 AI 需求持續帶動台灣經濟出口表
現強勁，預計出口商後續拋匯需求將持續提供台幣支撐。綜合以上，預估本週台幣
區間震盪。

＜預估本週＞ 美元兌台幣：31.600-32.000

美元兌人民幣匯率走勢  
Oct/5/2026 
＜上週走勢＞ 
上週人民幣震盪偏升。隨著美中兩國完成新一輪談判，雙方同意延長休兵，從
原定的 11 月延長到明年 1 月 ， 降低短期貿易摩擦升溫的風險 ， 支撐人民幣的走勢由
6.7170 走升至 6.7030；不過由於美伊戰事持續 ，國際油價持穩於每桶 100 美元上方 ，
加以美元指數及美債收益率雙雙走高 ， 令人民幣由升轉貶至6.7200 上方 ； 臨近週末，
美國公布 9 月非農數據降溫，聯準會10 月升息預期下滑，美元指數回落，境外人民
幣走升至 6.7000 週內高點，終場境外人民幣收在 6.7061，週線升值 166 點，境內人
民幣於十一長假前夕收盤於在 6.7053，週線升值 72 點。

＜未來分析＞ 
    預估本週人民幣區間震盪 。 就國際市場來看 ， 美國 9 月非農就業人數新增 2.9 萬
人，遠低於市場預期 9 萬人，且失業率由 4.1%上升至 4.2%，顯示就業市場動能略
為減弱。數據公布後，市場對於聯準會10 月升息的預期下降，美元指數小幅回落，
有助於人民幣的表現；就中國國內市場來看，人行中間價格再度升破 6.7400，有助
於人民幣的走勢 ， 不過10/1~10/7 是中國十一長假 ， 預計人民幣走勢主要將受國際美
元影響；最後就技術面來看，USD/CNH 日線圖中，各天期均線仍向下發散，空頭格
局不變，不過匯價反彈至 20 日均線附近，顯示下行動能暫緩。綜合以上，預估本週
人民幣區間震盪。

＜預估本週＞ 美元兌人民幣：6.6900-6.7300

＜上週走勢＞ 
上週 USD/JPY 震盪上行。週初，美元指數持穩在 101 以上高位，USD/JPY 大致
在 156.50 至 157.85 間波動；週間，適逢日本上半年會計年度結算，出口商拋匯使資
金匯回力道強勁 ， 帶動USD/JPY 下行最低來到 156.35，但逢低有買盤進場，USD/JPY
回到 157 整數關卡之上，後隨美債殖利率創高、美元指數突破 102 關卡，USD/JPY
上行最高來至 158.45；臨近週末，美國公布 9 月非農就業人數低於預期，USD/JPY
一度回落至 157 下方，終場收在 157.83，週線上漲 57 點。 
＜未來分析＞ 
  預估本週 USD/JPY 區間震盪 ， 主要原因如下 ： 一 、日本 9 月製造業 PMI 由 54.9
回落至 54.1，增長速度放慢至六個月以來的低位，主因為產出和新訂單增長放緩，
經濟復甦力道可能減緩，不利日圓表現。二、美國 8 月核心 PCE 物價指數年增 3.0%，
美國 9 月非農數據 2.9 萬人均低於預期，通膨和勞動力市場略有降溫使市場對聯準
會升息預期下滑，加之部分聯準會官員表示無需急於進一步升息，Fedwatch 顯示 10
月升息機率降至兩成，然而美國第二季 GDP 上修至 2.2%經濟基本面穩健仍將支撐
美元。三、就技術面而言，日線圖中，USD/JPY 匯價處於區間波動，且各天期均線
趨平，上方 158.50 有年線、季線壓力，下方 156.25 則有月線支撐，預期短線以區間
震盪可能性較高。綜合以上，預估 USD/JPY 本週呈現區間震盪。

美元兌歐元匯率走勢

＜上週走勢＞ 
  上週 EUR/USD 下跌。週初，美元指數持穩在 101 以上高位，EUR/USD 震盪下
行至 1.1310，而在紐約聯儲主席威廉姆斯發表不急於再次升息的偏鴿言論後，
EUR/USD 反彈至 1.1340；週間，美國 8 月 PCE 數據低於預期，EUR/USD 一度反彈
至 1.1380，然而在全球債券拋售潮下，資金回流美元帶動美元指數突破 102 關卡，
EUR/USD 跌破 1.1300 關卡最低來至 1.1214；臨近週末，美國 9 月非農就業人數低
於預期，EUR/USD 反彈至 1.1285 後回落，終場收在 1.1253，週線下跌 138 點。 
＜未來分析＞ 
預估本週 EUR/USD 震盪偏弱，主要原因如下：一、上週公佈歐元地區消費者
物價調和指數(HICP)，法國年增 3.4%、德國年增 3.3%、歐元區年增 3.8%，顯示能
源價格衝擊依然使通膨壓力居高不下， 市場預期歐洲央行可能在 12 月再度升息 ， 將
帶給歐元部分支撐。二、美國 8 月核心 PCE 物價指數年增 3.0%、月增 0.2%低於預
期，而 9 月非農就業人數 2.9 萬人低於預期的 9 萬人 ， 失業率則由 4.1%上升至 4.2%，
就業降溫及通膨回落降低市場對聯準會十月升息的預期，然而第二季美國 GDP 終
值從 1.5%上修至 2.2%，經濟基本面持穩仍有利美元維持強勢。三、技術面而言，在
日線圖中，EUR/USD 匯價在各天期均線下方並跌破 1.1300 整數關卡，月線向下穿
過季線形成死亡交叉，顯示短線空方動能較強。綜合以上，預估 EUR/USD 本週呈
現震盪偏弱的可能性較高。

公司簡介 
聯發科為台系 IC 設計龍頭廠商，為全球第
五大 IC 設計業者 (僅次於 Qualcomm、
Broadcom、nVidia)，著墨智慧型手機、智
慧裝置與電源管理晶片的發展。

思、Broadcom、Marvell、聯詠、瑞昱

王文雯 wenwen.wang@sinopac.com 
本次報告：法說會

聯發科 (2454 TT) 
ASIC 業務引領營運成長

永豐觀點 
ASIC 業務營收規模優於預期，2028 年還有第二個 ASIC 客戶步入量產(屬於 AI 機
櫃設計等級，公司能承接的工作範圍更多)，帶動營運顯著成長。 
 投資評價與建議 
維持買進：ASIC 業務貢獻優於預期，研究部上修獲利，預估聯發科 2026 年稅後
EPS 72.8 元(原預估 70.5 元)，2027 年稅後 EPS 142.78 元(原預估 132.52 元)，
2028 年稅後 EPS 321.22 元(原預估 312.93 元)。聯發科 ASIC 業務進展優於預期，
可望帶動後續營運強勁成長，研究部對聯發科維持買進，目標價由5570 元調升至
5800 元(由 2027~2028 年 EPS 均值與 25 倍評價)。

ESG 評析 
聯發科企業永續評鑑整體為 A 等，於 SASB 產業永續議題與跨產業五大永續面向
平均得分分別為 A 等與 A 等。

2

聯發科為 台系 IC 設計龍頭廠商 ：聯發科為全球第五大 IC 設計業者 (僅次於
Qualcomm、Broadcom、nVidia)，著墨智慧型手機、智慧裝置與電源管理晶片的
發展 。 在智慧型手機晶片方面的主要競爭對手為Qualcomm、 展訊及華為海思等 ，
在智慧家庭、AIoT 等應用領域則以 Broadcom、Marvell、聯詠及瑞昱等廠商為
主。客戶主要透過代理商供應產品，終端客戶以全球手機品牌(OPPO、VIVO、小
米、Samsung、榮耀等)以及智慧家電品牌為主。 
3Q26 財報優於預期 ：聯發科 3Q26 營收 1680.58 億元(+10%QoQ，+18%YoY)，
毛利率 46.3%， 營業利益率16.1%， 稅後淨利278.82 億元(+15%QoQ，+11%YoY)，
稅後 EPS 17.37 元，財報優於預期，主要是營收超標、營業費用控管得宜所致。 
聯發科 3Q26 財報各個細項表現如下：(1) 營收季增 10%，超逾目標值 1522 億元
~1598 億元(QoQ+0%~+5%)，三大產品線表現均優於預期，得益於專案挹注程
度與供應鏈產能爭取進度較佳，三大產品線以Smart Edge Platform 挹注最顯著。
聯發科 3Q26 三 大 產 品 線 營 運 動 能 如 下 ：Smart edge platform 季增
20%(+68%YoY， 營收佔比58%)、 電源晶片(+1%QoQ，+18%YoY， 營收佔比5%)、
手機營收滑落 1%QoQ(-17%YoY，營收佔比 37%)，其中 Smart edge platform
季增幅度超逾公司原預期的，QoQ+5%~+9%，公司前次法說即已表示將有多項
聯網與車載項目於 3Q26 進入量產 ， 現在看來5G modem/AI tablet(天璣 9500 導
入三星平板電腦 Tab S120/AI PC / 車載晶片貢獻更優，並且 TV SoC 內含 DRAM
漲價亦有貢獻。 電源晶片亦優於公司原預期的持平， 主要是來自運算與資料中心市
占率提升。手機僅季減1%，落於公司原預期的 QoQ-5%~+0%的上緣，應受惠於
旗艦晶片天璣 9600 出台所致。(2) 毛利率 46.3%，與前季相仿，落於目標值區間
44.5%~47.5%區間。(3) 營業費用 507 億元(+7%QoQ)，營業費用成長性<營收
成長性，反映營業費用控管得宜。(4) 聯發科 3Q26 存貨 1535 億元(+56%QoQ)，
庫存天數由 2Q26 的 102 天增至 127 天，主要是因應 ASIC 業務拓展。

3

4Q26 營收展望優於預期：聯發科預估 4Q26 營收 2101 億元~2218 億元(以美元
對新台幣匯率 31.7 元計算)，QoQ+25%~+32%，YoY+40% ~+48%，毛利率目
標值區間下滑 1%至 43.5%~46.5%，營業費用率 24%~28%，反映營業利益率
16%~23%，優於過往三年多落於 14%~19%水準。聯發科 4Q26 展望細項如下：
(1) 4Q26 營運由 ASIC 業務驅動，(2) 4Q26 毛利率目標值區間下滑 1%，主要是受
ASIC 業務毛利率<公司均值，以及晶片價格因製造成本上升而出現轉嫁事宜的綜
合因素影響。(3) ASIC 業務的營業利益率>公司均值，故帶動公司 4Q26 營業利益
率回升。(4) 公司預期 2026 年營收成長性，超逾原預期的 YoY+5%~+9%。研究
部預估聯發科 4Q26 營收 2180 億元(＋30%QoQ，+45%YoY)，毛利率 45%，稅
後獲利 405.2 億元(+45%QoQ，+77%YoY)，稅後 EPS 25.25 元。 
ASIC 業務貢獻值優於預期：聯發科本次法說揭示 ASIC 業務貢獻值優於預期：(1) 
公司預期 2026 年 ASIC 業務 25 億美元(優於前次法說僅預期 >20 億美元)，
Zebrafish 已在 09/2026 開始小量出貨。(2) 2027 年 ASIC 業務 160 億美元(市佔
率 20%)，落於公司原預期的市占率 15%~20%與貢獻 120 億~160 億美元的上
緣。(3) 第二款 ASIC Humufish 維持預計將於 1Q28 步入量產，目前該平台部分
die 業已 tape out 完成，主要 die 也在 tape out 進行當中，先進封裝仍將優先採
用 Intel EMIB 。(4) 2027 年的營業利益率將>20%，反映營運槓桿效益提升。(5) 
第三代 ASIC(若順利應於 2029~2030 貢獻)則目前尚未定義最終規格。 
再者，公司亦表示第二個 ASIC 業務(搭載 NVLink Fusion) 預計 2028 年開始出貨
(前季法說僅表示尚與潛在客戶洽談中，本季已明確表示將於 2028 年出貨)，此為
新客戶(公司表示非中國客戶，故應表示該客戶非先前傳言的 Bytedance)。協助單
顆晶片設計與進入 NVLink Fusion 機櫃級設計的差異，在於幫客戶將晶片納入整
合進 Nvidia 的 AI 機櫃系統，需要增加高速互聯/相容性/記憶體架構/封裝協同設
計，以與 Nvidia 的交換器或網路系統偕同運作，反映聯發科能承接的工作範圍更
多，亦即接到單一客戶的商業價值應該更多。但因為此為聯發科首次導入 AI 機櫃
等級的業務，相關客戶與細項內容，均尚難知曉，故研究部本次財務預估尚未將此
估算在內。 
維持買進：考量 ASIC 業務貢獻優於預期，研究部上修獲利，預估聯發科 2026 年
營收 6874 億元(+15%YoY)，毛利率 45.87%，營業利率 16.9%，稅後獲利 1168
億元(+11%YoY)，稅後 EPS 72.8 元(原預估 70.5 元)，2027 年營收 1.17 兆元
(+71%YoY)， 毛利率43.77%， 營業利率21.5%， 稅後獲利2292 億元(+96%YoY)，

### Page 1

MediaTek

TPU wins on track for steep ramp through 
’28 joined by further CSP/AI lab potential

Reiterate Rating: BUY | PO: 6,200 TWD | Price: 3,915 TWD

3Q26 beat on consumer builds; 4Q26 lifted by TPU ramp 
3Q26 sales were +10% QoQ on its flagship mobile SoC launch and customers’ pre-builds 
ahead of its chipset price hike on rising manufacturing cost. On higher sales and better 
mix, GM/OpM edged up to 46%/16%, taking 3Q26 EPS to NT$17.5 above BofAe/street. 
4Q26 sales was guided +25-32% QoQ, in line with BofA preview, as steep TPUv8t ramp 
now tracking to US$2.5bn (vs. US$2bn guide before) partially balanced by soft demand 
elsewhere (-15-22% QoQ vs. 5-year seasonal +1%) following the early pull-in. Despite 
TPUv8t growing to ~35% of its sales at lower margins, GMs is guided resiliently at 45% 
in 4Q26, implying better margins for its merchant business at 47%. Fast sales scale-up 
and stable GMs should lift operating leverage with OpMs guided for 19% at midpoint. 
ASIC pipeline spanning across CSPs and frontier AI labs 
Mediatek sees TPUv8t ramping to US$16bn+ revenue in ’27, lifting OpM to 20%+, with 
upside if it could secure more capacity. The follow on TPUv9 is tracking for a meaningful 
production potential in ’28. Importantly, it confirmed a 2nd ASIC customer win in design 
now and production contribution set to kick off in ’28. We believe this new project would 
require Mediatek’s component sourcing and supply chain management at a much higher 
dollar content. This along with potential Bytedance win could add upside to our base 
case for its data center revenue of US$19bn/US$53bn in '27/'28 with xAI ASIC revenue 
joined from 2H28 to supplement Google TPU upgrades.  
AI project diversification supports earnings and valuation 
As ASIC business inflects, we lift our 2026 EPS to NT$74.13 and keep ‘27/28 EPS at 
NT$168.93/404.32. Keep our Buy rating and NT$6,200 PO on 24x 2H27-1H28E P/E 
(implied 30x ASIC EPS + 10x merchant EPS), backed by sales/EPS 90%/134% CAGR. 
Growing AI project wins should alleviate the concerns on customer concentration and 
TPU competition debate with share downside protected by 23x/10x ‘27/28 straight P/E.

Company Sector 
Semiconductors

Company Description 
MediaTek is a fabless IC design company supplying to 
wireless communications and digital consumer markets. 
Before taking leadership in mobile SoC area, it had built 
solid ground in the TV SoC and optical disk drive markets. 
The firm invests heavily in R&D to seek better positioning 
in the 5G cycle, and has a complete offering including ASIC, 
Wi-Fi, PMIC etc. to address applications beyond 
smartphone, such as IoT and auto.

Investment Rationale 
We have Buy rating on MediaTek as we think MediaTek is 
ready and has the strength to brace for the upcoming 
proliferation of IoT devices and opportunities in growing 
markets like ASIC, ARM-based PC, auto, and etc., and we 
believe its solid balance sheet and cashflow could provide 
downside support to the share price. Meanwhile, we expect 
5G smartphone penetration to be a multi-year theme for 
the company, with its better competitive landscape in 
duopoly 5G era and an improved margin profile.

### Page 4

Price objective basis & risk 
MediaTek (MDTKF) 
Our PO of NT$6,200 is based on 24x 2H27-1H28 P/E. which is towards the higher end 
of its historical P/E range of 8-25x, as we believe the company's broadening ASIC 
pipeline could drive an earnings upgrade cycle.

Upside risks to our PO are: (1) better than expected smartphone demand, especially in 
China, (2) a better pricing in mobile communication segment thanks to alleviated 
competition and rising exposure to high-end/flagship market, and (3) an even more 
superior investment return on non-op side to uplift its net cash position. (4) Unexpected 
collaboration with global technology leaders leads to higher than expected 
margins/profits

Downside risks to our PO are: (1) weaker-than-expected end-market situation in 
consumer-related smartphone/TV space, (2) macro challenges including inflation/policy-
related pressures, (3) rising competition from existing players and/or newcomers in 
smartphone/TV SoC (system on chip) space, and (4) ASP/margin pressure caused by each 
or all of the above and/or rising foundry costs. (4) Bidding process for Meta Inference 
ASIC is slower than expected.

FUNDAMENTAL EQUITY OPINION KEY: Opinions include a Volatility Risk Rating, an Investment Rating and an Income Rating.  VOLATILITY RISK RATINGS, indicators 
of potential price fluctuation, are: A - Low, B - Medium and C - High. INVESTMENT RATINGS reflect the analyst’s assessment of both a stock’s absolute total return 
potential as well as its attractiveness for investment relative to other stocks within its Coverage Cluster (defined below). Our investment ratings are: 1 - Buy stocks 
are expected to have a total return of at least 10% and are the most attractive stocks in the coverage cluster; 2 - Neutral stocks are expected to remain flat or 
increase in value and are less attractive than Buy rated stocks and 3 - Underperform stocks are the least attractive stocks in a coverage cluster. An investment 
rating of 6 (No Rating) indicates that a stock is no longer trading on the basis of fundamentals. Analysts assign investment ratings considering, among other things, 
the 0-12 month total return expectation for a stock and the firm’s guidelines for ratings dispersions (shown in the table below). The current price objective for a 
stock should be referenced to better understand the total return expectation at any given time. The price objective reflects the analyst’s view of the potential price 
appreciation (depreciation).

Greater China Technology Hardware
Asia Pacific
Industry View In-Line
(-) PC OEMs and ODMs: Intel (covered by Joseph Moore) expects 2H26 PC units to
fall 20%+ y/y as OEMs aggressively cut inventories amid weaker sell-through; the
correction has accelerated over the past two months. This aligns with Morgan

Stanley's estimate at a ~25% y/y decline  (report link).
(+) Server OEMs and ODMs: Intel guides for server CPU unit growth of 30%+ in
both 2026 and 2027, with upside if wafer/substrate supply improves. It sees server
CPU TAM rising from US$40bn today to >US$200bn in five years, driven mainly by
AI agents.
(+) ABF substrates:  Most of the previously discussed ~40% capacity increase is due
in 2H27; Intel expects key component purchase commitments to rise sharply over

the next several quarters. Intel is also prioritizing securing substrate capacity and
improving yields for the EMIB-T. Complex designs add yield risk, which could lower
effective output, worsening ABF substrate shortages but adding further support for
pricing.

Key Takeaways
We view negative PC comments as more bearish for OEMs and ODMs positioned
in the lower end consumer segment, like Acer and Compal.
Strong CPU unit growth would be positive for Wiwynn, Lenovo, and Lotes.
Tighter ABF substrate supply and stronger pricing would be positive for
Unimicron and NYPCB's revenue and margins.

STOCK RATINGS
Morgan Stanley uses a relative rating system using terms such as Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated or Underweight (see definitions below). Morgan Stanley does not assign ratings of Buy,
Hold or Sell to the stocks we cover. Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated and Underweight are not the equivalent of buy, hold and sell.  Investors should carefully read the definitions of all
ratings used in Morgan Stanley Research. In addition, since Morgan Stanley Research contains more complete information concerning the analyst's views, investors should carefully read Morgan

Stanley Research, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone.  In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice.  An investor's decision
to buy or sell a stock should depend on individual circumstances (such as the investor's existing holdings) and other considerations.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

26.3Q Review: 수익성에 대한 입증이 필요  Company Brief

26.3Q Review: 매출액 31.6조원, 영업이익 1.8조원  
기아는 26.3Q 매출액 31.6조원 (+10.1% YoY, -4.4% QoQ), 영업이익 1.8조원 
(+24.3% YoY, -30.8% QoQ, +5.8% OPM) 등의 실적을 기록했다. 26.3Q 영업이익은 
시장 컨센서스 (2.36조원)를 22.9% 하회했다 [iM증권 추정치: 매출액 31.5조원, 
영업이익 2.3조원]. 주요 구성 요소별 내용은 아래와 같다. 
① P: 26.3Q 26.3Q 전동화 차량 판매는 30.0만대 (+47% YoY, 판매 비중 
36.2%)를 기록했는데, 이 중 HEV 17.2 만대 (+46% YoY), EV 12.3 만대 
(+75% YoY)로 구성됨. 미국 HEV 판매가 전년 동기 대비 118% 증가하며 
시장 성장률을 약 4배 상회했고, 특히 서유럽 EV 판매가 전년 대비 163% 
증가함. 단 26.3Q 중 원화가 빠르게 강세로 전환되며 원/달러, 원/유로 
평균 환율은 전분기 대비 각 5.2%, 6.2% 하락함에 따른 – 효과 관찰됨. 
② Q: 26.3Q 도매 판매는 84.7만대 (+7.9% YoY) 를 기록했고, 북미, 유럽, 
인도 등 주요 시장에서 고른 성장 나타남. 26.3Q 소매 판매 또한 82.9만대 
(+6.9% YoY)로 글로벌 산업 수요 (-6% YoY)를 상회. 북미는 HEV, 내수와 
유럽은 EV, 인도는 신형 셀토스와 소넷을 중심으로 판매가 확대됨 (이하 
26.3Q 권역별 도매 판매 YoY, 국내: +2.2%, 북미: +7.6%. 유럽: +19.3%. 
인도: +38.5%). 이란 사태 장기화로 아중동 도매 판매는 전년 동기 대비 
28.4% 감소했으나, 수요가 견조한 중남미와 CIS로 물량을 전환하며 
감소분을 상당 부분 만회함. 연간 도매 판매 가이던스 (335만대)를 유지함. 
③ C: 26.3Q 관세 비용은 약 0.7조원으로 추정되며, 전년 동기 대비 
5,000억원 감소함 (25.2Q 0.7조원, 25.3Q 1.2조원, 25.4Q 1.0조원, 26.1Q 
0.8조원). 반면 인센티브 증가 및 가격 인하 7,270억원, 서유럽 EV 비중 
확대에 따른 믹스 악화 4,650억원, 원자재 가격 상승 영향 3,680억원 등 
총 1.6조원의 감익 요인이 발생함. 이에 26.3Q 매출원가율은 84.1%로 
전년 동기 대비 3.0%p 상승함 (인센티브 +1.9%p, 재료비 +1.2%p). 다만 
26.3Q 기말 환율 급락하며 판매보증충당부채 환입 규모가 축소되었고 
판매보증비 약 9,000억원 감소. 판매관리비율은 10.1%로 3.7%p 개선됨. 
26.3Q 기아 순이익은 1.29조원으로 전년 동기 대비 9.6% 감소했다. 원화 강세에 
따라 외환환산 관련 이익이 전년 동기 540억원 이익에서 3,718억원으로 손실 
전환됐고, 현대모비스 등 관계사 이익 감소로 지분법손익 또한 360억원 감소했기 
때문이다 (현대차 순이익 감소 논리와 동일). 1) 산업 수요 감소와 중동 판매 차질에도 
권역별 파워트레인 대응을 통해 그룹사 대비 가지고 있는 외형 성장성을 증명했으나, 
2) 유럽 EV 판매 확대 과정에서 발생한 인센티브와 믹스 악화라는 비용 부담, 원자재 
가격 상승이 관세 감소 효과를 대부분 반납했고, 3) 분기 중 급격한 원화 강세가 
더해지며 시장 기대를 크게 하회하는 수익성을 기록했다는 것이 26.3Q 실적 총평이다.

실적 발표 후 주가 급락: 성장에도 불구하고 수익성에 대한 우려  
26.10.30 실적 발표 당일 기아 주가는 11.7% 하락했다. 하루 전 발표된 그룹사 실적 
발표와 상반된 모습이었다. 시장이 이번 실적에서 확인한 우려는 아래와 같다. 첫째, 
성장과 이익의 괴리다. 관세 영향을 제외한 26.3Q 영업이익은 2.52조원으로 전년 
동기(2.70조원) 대비 오히려 1,770억원 감소했다. 판매 증가에 따른 물량 효과 
(+4,420억원)와 환율 효과 (+5,290억원)가 발생했음에도, 인센티브와 믹스 악화로 이를 
대부분 반납한 것이다. 기아의 외형 성장 상당 부분이 유럽 EV 판매 증가에서 나오는데, 
해당 시장에서는 중국 EV 업체와의 경쟁으로 공격적인 프로모션이 불가피하다. 26.3Q 
유럽 권역에서의 중소형 EV 판매가 사업 계획을 크게 웃돌며 EV 믹스는 13.1%에서 
14.8%로 확대됐다. 시장은 이를 M/S를 위해 수익성을 훼손하고 있다고 받아들이는 
것이다. 둘째, 당장 26.4Q에도 유의미한 수익성 개선이 없을 것이라는 점이다. 기아는 
26.3Q 컨퍼런스 콜에서 4Q 영업이익률이 3Q 수준과 크게 다르지 않을 것이며, 연초 
제시한 연간 가이던스 (영업이익 10.2조원, +8.3% OPM) 에 다소 미달할 것이라고 
밝혔다. 중동 분쟁의 영향이 최소 연말까지 이어지고, 원자재와 물류비 부담 또한 
지속될 것이 회사의 판단이다. 무엇보다 원/달러 환율이 26.2Q 평균 약 1,505원에서 
26.3Q 약 1,430원, 현재 1,320~1,340원 수준까지 하락하며 이에 따른 - 영향이 커질 
수 있다. 셋째, 그룹사와의 이익 체력 비교다. 현대차는 26.3Q 파업과 생산 차질로 도매 
판매가 11.3% 감소했음에도 원가 절감을 통해 영업이익이 5.5% 증가했고, 시장 
기대를 상회했다. 반면 기아는 판매가 7.9% 증가했음에도 영업이익이 컨센서스를 20% 
이상 하회했다. 26.3Q 양사 영업이익 격차는 약 8,600억원까지 벌어진 상황이다.  
단기 노이즈를 제외한다면 기아의 기업 가치는 여전히 높다  
그럼에도 당사는 기아의 EV 전략, 낮은 수익성을 이유로 중장기 기업 가치에 대한 
기대를 낮출 이유는 없다고 판단한다. 첫째, 외부 요인을 걷어내면 본원적인 수익 
창출력은 유지되고 있다. 기아는 26.3Q 영업이익률을 낮춘 외부 요인을 원자재 1.2%p, 
중동 판매 차질 0.7%p, 환율 1.7%p 등 총 3.6%p로 분석했다. 즉 중동 분쟁이 
해소되면 상당 부분이 사라질 일회성 요인이다. 둘째, EV 수익성 개선 경로는 HEV가 
걸어온 길과 같다. 기아는 HEV 역시 도입 초기에는 수익을 내지 못했으나 현재는 ICE에 
버금가는 수익성을 내고 있다며, 26~27 년 현재의 EV 전략을 유지하겠다고 밝혔다. 
유럽 ICE 시장은 26.3Q 전년 동기 대비 약 20% 축소된 반면 EV 시장은 50% 이상 
성장하고 있다. 전환기에 스케일을 확보하지 못하면 EV 마진을 개선할 방법도 없다. 
기아는 향후 3년간 배터리, 모터, 히트펌프 등 주요 부품에서 연 10% 수준의 원가 
절감을 추진하고, 2~3년 내 EV를 HEV와 같은 수익성 경로에 올려놓겠다는 계획이다. 
셋째, 중장기 성장 계획을 수정하지 않았다. 기아는 27년 사업 계획이 CID 당시 제시한 
숫자와 큰 차이가 없을 것이며, 중장기 영업이익률 10% 목표도 유지했다. IAA 시행에 
대비해 슬로바키아 공장에서 EV를 전량 생산하고 ICE 국내 수출 방안도 검토 중이다. 
주주환원 측면에서도 TSR 35% 를 지키고, 자사주 매입보다 배당을 우선하겠다는 
입장이다. 10/30 종가와 최소 DPS 기준만으로도 배당수익률은 6.3%에 달하고 있다.

기아 판매관리비 (좌) 판매관리비율 (우)
  26.4Q 기아 매출액 31.8조원, 영업이익 2.2조원 전망 
기아 26.4Q 실적을 매출액 31.8조원 (+13.2% YoY), 영업이익 2.2조원 (+20.8% YoY, 
+7.0% OPM)으로 전망한다. 26.4Q 판매 성장세는 이어지고, 3Q에 집중 반영된 원자재 
부담 일부 완화와 계절적 성수기 효과로 3Q 대비 수익성은 소폭 개선될 것이다. 다만 
1) 원화 강세에 따른 평균 환율 하락, 2) 유럽 EV 인센티브의 높은 수준 유지 (미국은 
할부 금리 상승으로 QoQ 약 200달러 증가, 유럽은 전분기와 유사), 3) 중동 판매 
차질과 원자재, 물류비 부담 지속을 감안하면 의미 있는 반등은 어렵다. 이는 4Q 
영업이익률이 3Q 수준과 크게 다르지 않을 것이라는 회사의 언급과도 궤를 같이한다. 
기아 목표주가 150,000원으로 하향  
기아 목표주가를 26.4Q 추정치 하향을 반영해 150,000원으로 하향한다. 기아의 판매 
경쟁력은 금번 분기에도 증명됐다. 다만 EV 판매 확대가 수익성 개선으로 연결된다는 
점이 숫자로 확인되기 전까지, 시장은 기아의 성장에 할인율을 적용할 것이다. 중동 
분쟁 해소와 EV 원가 절감이 맞물리는 27년이 기아가 수익성을 증명할 시점이 될 
것이며, 6%를 상회하는 배당수익률이 그때까지 주가의 하방을 지지할 것으로 판단된다.

Compliance notice 
당 보고서 공표일 기준으로 해당 기업과 관련하여, 
 회사는 해당 종목을 1%이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다.  
 금융투자분석사와 그 배우자는 해당 기업의 주식을 보유하고 있지 않습니다.  
 당 보고서는 기관투자가 및 제 3자에게 E-mail등을 통하여 사전에 배포된 사실이 없습니다.  
 회사는 6개월간 해당 기업의 유가증권 발행과 관련 주관사로 참여하지 않았습니다. 
 당 보고서에 게재된 내용들은 본인의 의견을 정확하게 반영하고 있으며, 외부의 부당한 압력이나 간섭 없이 작성되었음을 확인합니다. 
본 분석자료는 투자자의 증권투자를 돕기 위한 참고자료이며, 따라서, 본 자료에 의한 투자자의 투자결과에 대해 어떠한 목적의 증빙자료로도 사용될 수 없으며, 어떠한 경우에도 작성자 및 
당사의 허가 없이 전재, 복사 또는 대여될 수 없습니다. 무단전재 등으로 인한 분쟁발생시 법적 책임이 있음을 주지하시기 바랍니다.

[투자의견]

Source PDF: all report pdf/[000660] SK하이닉스(000660) HBM 하나로 시가총액 설명SK하이닉스(000660) HBM 하나로 시가총액 설명HBM 하나로 시가총액 설명 - 기업.pdf
Report source: Hankyung

### Page 1

반도체(Overweight): Stress Test  
90 
SK하이닉스  (000660) 
2026.11.04

HBM 하나로 시가총액 설명 
[반도체/전기전자] 고의영 
2122-9179 ey.ko@imfnsec.com

업황 우려에 고유 할인까지 겹친 과도한 하락 
투자의견 Buy, 목표주가 230만원으로 커버리지를 개시한다. ‘27 년 BPS에 목표 
P/B 2.3배를 적용했다[표1]. 이는 상장 이후 평균 P/B의 +2표준편차에 해당한다. 
‘26~’28년 ROE는 각각 102%, 66%, 47%로, ‘26년 정점 이후에도 과거 업사이클 
고점보다 높은 수준이 유지될 전망이다.  
한편, 이익 지속성에 대한 믿음이 공고해져 시장의 시야가 ‘28년으로 향할 경우 
‘28년 BPS 기준 목표주가는 360만원까지 열릴 수 있다[표2]. 
동사 주가는 ‘26년 6월 고점 대비 10월 저점 기준 57% 하락해 과거 다운사이클 
평균 하락폭인 -51%를 넘어섰고, 과거 8~13개월에 걸쳐 진행됐던 조정이 
이번에는 4개월 만에 나타났다. 시장이 ‘28년 이익 급감을 상정하고 있는 것이다. 
그러나 당사는 아래와 같은 투자포인트와 향후 주가 흐름을 제시한다.

‘28년 HBM 가치, 시가총액의 1.3배 
HBM 비중이 높아질수록 동사의 이익 변동성은 낮아질 것이다. 특히 cHBM으로 
갈수록 고객별 수주생산 제품으로 현물시장과 유통 재고가 없고, 생산에 앞서 
물량과 가격이 결정된다. 동사는 이 전환이 업계에서 가장 선두권이다.  
당사는 동사 HBM 매출이 ‘26년 43조원에서 ‘28년 183조원으로 증가할 것으로 
전망한다. 만약, 동사의 ‘26~’28년 HBM 영업이익에 시기별 TSMC P/E(2 1배, 
15배, 1 2배)를 적용하면 HBM 가치는 동사 시가총액의 47%, 93%, 135%를 
설명한다[표3]. ‘27 년에 이미 HBM 하나만으로 현재 시가총액이 설명된다는 
의미다. 시장이 통상적으로 받아들이는 멀티플 10배를 타겟팅해도 결과는 크게 
다르지 않다.

150만원에 사면 ‘30년 EPS 2배 
동사의 주주환원은 자사주 매입/소각 중심일 것으로 상정한다. 당사는 동사가 현 
정책 기간(‘25~’27년)에 이어 ‘28년 이후에도 FCF의 50%를 환원하고 이 중 
70%를 자사주 매입/소각에 쓸 것으로 가정했다.  
당사 가정대로라면 총주주환원수익률은 현 시가총액 대비 ‘27년 14%, ‘28~’30년 
연 17~24%다[표4]. 
관건은 매입 단가다. 자사주 매입의 주당가치 개선 효과는 P/E가 낮을수록 
커지는데, 현 주가의 ‘27년 P/E는 2.7배다. 현 주가 수준인 150만원에 매년 균등 
매입한다고 ‘30년까지 주식수는 50% 줄어든다.   
그 결과 ‘30년 주당순이익은 매입이 없을 때 대비 2배가 된다[표4]. ‘28년 이후 
이익이 정체되더라도 주당 이익은 계속 늘어나는 구조이며, 이익 지속성에 대한 
의심이 주가를 누를수록 소각 효과는 커진다. 현 주가 수준의 정체가 장기화되기 
어려운 이유다.

‘27년 주가 경로: 회복, 박스권, 재평가 
연말까지는 급락 이후 저점을 높여가는 회복 구간이다. 솔리다임 상장 철회에 이어 3분기 
실적발표에서 ‘26년 주주환원 100조원과 매 분기 자사주 매입 방침이 제시되면서 환원 
의지는 검증되었다. 
‘27년 상반기까지는 ‘28년 불확실성에 대한 우려가 상존할 전망이다. 가격 하락폭은 LTA 
방어력이 확인되기 전까지 블랙박스다. 이 불확실성이 멀티플을 누르는 동안 ‘27년 이익은 
계속 상향되므로 EPS 상승과 멀티플 하락이 상쇄되는 구간이 이어질 전망이다. 이 구간의 
기회비용은 환원으로 보상받는다. 
‘27년 하반기 이후에는 실적 추정의 기준 연도가 ‘28년으로 넘어가고, 신규 팹 물량이 소량 
출하되면서 센티먼트상 우려는 커질 것이다. 그러나 당사는 2H27에 ‘28년 HBM 협의와 
LTA 연장이 지속적으로 확인되면서 이익이 스트레스 테스트 범위 안에서 유지될 것으로 
전망하며[그림2], 이것이 숫자로 확인되는 시점에 순환 할인이 축소되고 재평가가 나타날 
것으로 판단한다.

업사이드 요인 
① AI 투자 지속성 우려로 최근 주가 하락이 가팔랐던 만큼, 이 부분에 대한 우려가 
완화되면 반등의 속도와 폭이 예상보다 빠를 수 있다 
② 향후 주주환원 규모가 예상보다 더 적극적일 경우 추가적인 업사이드가 될 수 있다. 
혹은 주주환원 이후에도 남는 대규모 FCF의 활용 방안도 촉매가 될 수 있다.

Compliance notice 
당 보고서 공표일 기준으로 해당 기업과 관련하여, 
 회사는 해당 종목을 1%이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다.  
 금융투자분석사와 그 배우자는 해당 기업의 주식을 보유하고 있지 않습니다.  
 당 보고서는 기관투자가 및 제 3자에게 E-mail등을 통하여 사전에 배포된 사실이 없습니다.  
 회사는 6개월간 해당 기업의 유가증권 발행과 관련 주관사로 참여하지 않았습니다. 
 당 보고서에 게재된 내용들은 본인의 의견을 정확하게 반영하고 있으며, 외부의 부당한 압력이나 간섭 없이 작성되었음을 확인합니다. 
본 분석자료는 투자자의 증권투자를 돕기 위한 참고자료이며, 따라서, 본 자료에 의한 투자자의 투자결과에 대해 어떠한 목적의 증빙자료로도 사용될 수 없으며, 어떠한 경우에도 작성자 및 
당사의 허가 없이 전재, 복사 또는 대여될 수 없습니다. 무단전재 등으로 인한 분쟁발생시 법적 책임이 있음을 주지하시기 바랍니다.

[투자의견] 
종목추천 투자등급  
종목투자의견은 향후 12개월간 추천일 종가대비 해당종목의 예상 목표수익률을 의미함.  
 Buy(매수): 추천일 종가대비 +15% 이상  
 Hold(보유): 추천일 종가대비 -15% ~ 15% 내외 등락 
 Sell(매도): 추천일 종가대비 -15% 이상

투자의견 BUY  
매수, 유지 
6개월 목표주가 500,000 
유지  
현재주가 
(26.10.29) 
231,000 
반도체업종

◼ 3분기 실적발표에 대한 시장의 반응은 다소 냉소적. 
◼ 주주환원은 시점의 문제일 뿐. 강화 방향성은 여전히 유효. 
◼ 메모리반도체 이익의 폭증이 가격의 매력을 유발. 비중확대 전략 유효.

매력적인 가격, 변하지 않은 주주환원 정책의 방향성 
목표주가와 매수의견 유지. 메모리반도체 주도 폭발적 이익 상승이 가격 (주
가)의 매력을 지속 유발하고 있고, 주주환원 정책의 강화 방향성에도 변한 것
은 없다는 판단. 절대적으로 싼 주가를 비중 확대의 기회로 삼을 필요. 
1. 주주환원, 시점의 문제 
금번 실적발표에 대한 시장의 반응은 다소 냉소적. 대외 불확실성이 큰 만큼, 
시장은 자사주 매입/소각과 같은 즉각적 주가 부양 정책의 발표를 기대.  
당사는 삼성전자의 주주환원이 “시점의 문제”라 생각. 총 주주환원의 규모도, 
차기 (2027-2029년) 주주환원 정책의 강화 방향에도 변한 것은 없다는 판
단. 시장이 기대하는 자사주 매입/소각은 우선주 중심으로 시행될 가능성이 
높으며, 시행 시, 그 규모는 25조원 내외일 것 (DPS 12,567원)으로 전망. 
2. 메모리반도체, 더 올라갈 것 
보수적 환율 전망 (2027년 평균 환율 1,300원 적용)에도, 2027년 메모리반도
체 영업이익은 608조원에 달할 것. 이익 전망의 상향 여력 또한 유효. 
1) 최대 생산, 최대 판매 
삼성전자는 금일 4Q26 DRAM 판매량 가이던스로 전분기 대비 10% 중후반 
성장 (q-q +18%로 추정)을 제시. 일부 투자자들은 높아진 기저 부담으로 
2027년 판매 성장이 제한될 것을 우려. 당사는 높아진 기저에도 2027년 
DRAM 판매가 전년대비 20% 상승할 것으로 전망. 
- 극단적 수급 불균형 속, 삼성전자는 “최대 생산, 최대 판매” 정책을 시행 
중. 삼성전자만의 강력한 Execution이 20% 대 성장을 유지시킬 원동력. 
- DRAM 1c 수율의 조기 개선 속, 범용 DRAM 양산에 DRAM 1c Capa가 일
부 투입될 것으로 예상. Bit 성장을 높일 수 있는 변화가 될 것. 
2) 열려 있는 가격의 상방 
1Q27 모바일 DRAM에서 Surprise가 발생할 것이라는 의견을 유지 (전분기 
대비 30% 이상 상승). 장기계약이 체결된 CSP향으로도 가격의 추가 상승 여
력이 존재한다는 점을 상기할 필요. CSP의 경우, 당장의 비용 부담보다 AI 시
장 선점을 우선시. 장기계약으로 형성된 가격 Band의 천장 (Ceiling) 또한 시
장 우려 대비 낮지 않음을 감안할 필요. 2027년 범용 DRAM ASP는 전년대비 
40% 중후반까지 상승할 가능성 (현 전망: 전년대비 34% 상승).  
3) HBM, 제품 경쟁력과 점유율의 동반 성장  
최근 삼성전자향으로 HBM4/HBM4e 추가 공급 요청이 쇄도하고 있는 것으
로 추정. 일부 고객은 연내 추가 공급 시 2027년 계약 가격을 선제 지급할 
의사를 표출. 제품 경쟁력이 회복되었음을 보여주는 증거라 생각. 
제품 경쟁력의 회복과 최대 Capa의 이점 속, 2027년은 HBM 점유율 1위를 
확보하는 기념비적 해가 될 것으로 전망 (매출액 기준 점유율 38% 예상).

Document 316: Could Midterms Give Wall Street A Cold Shower_

Source PDF: all report pdf/Could Midterms Give Wall Street A Cold Shower_.pdf
Report source: researchmail轉寄
Date: 2026-11-03
Pages: 9
Status: ok

### Page 1

From:    Yardeni QuickTakes <yardeni-research@ghost.io>
Subject: Could Midterms Give Wall Street A Cold Shower?
Date:    1 hour ago
Could Midterms Give Wall Street A Cold Shower?

YARDENI QUICKTAKES
Could Midterms Give Wall Street
A Cold Shower?
By Ed Yardeni & Elias Griepentrog • 2 Nov 2026
View in browser

I. When Gridlock Isn't Good for Stocks
The US midterm elections will be over tomorrow night. According to
betting markets, Democrats have roughly a 95% probability of winning the
House and a 76% probability of taking the Senate (charts).
We suspect that stock investors have yet to fully discount the possibility of
a Democratic sweep of Congress. Some may remain skeptical that polls
confirming strong support for Democrats are accurate. They might reckon

### Page 2

that Democrats are more likely than Republicans to talk to pollsters over
the phone.
In any event, Wall Street traditionally welcomes political gridlock because
it reduces the likelihood that Congress will pass extremist legislation. But
this time could be different for two reasons.
First, deepening partisanship could turn gridlock into damaging political
paralysis at a time when the Bond Vigilantes are already uneasy about
Washington's unsustainable fiscal trajectory. A Democratic sweep could

complicate negotiations over government spending and the debt ceiling.
Prolonged shutdowns and fiscal standoffs could push Treasury yields
higher, weighing on equity valuations.

### Page 3

Second, gridlock's traditional ability to constrain policymaking may not
hold this time. President Donald Trump has relied heavily on executive
authority, with some of his most market-disruptive policies, including tariffs
and military action against Iran, pursued largely without congressional
approval. A Democratic-controlled Congress may therefore do little to
restrain him. Moreover, with the midterms behind him, Trump may feel
even less constrained politically and more willing to pursue controversial

policies through executive action. Hence, a blue wave could deliver a cold
shower for stocks, though we would view a significant selloff as a buying
opportunity given the remarkable strength of the economy and corporate
earnings.
One encouraging development ahead of the elections was today's reports
that the US and Iran are working on a peaceful diplomatic solution to their
war. Iranian President Masoud Pezeshkian said he has granted his
government greater authority to pursue peace talks, while President

Trump and Vice President Vance also signaled progress. Hopes for a deal
sent the price of Brent crude oil down 7.6% to $104.77 per barrel (chart).
However, we have been here before with apparent diplomatic
breakthroughs. We still see a high risk of military re-escalation. The bid-
ask spread remains wide, with Tehran demanding sanctions relief, the
release of frozen assets, and an end to the US naval blockade of Iranian
ports, while Washington insists on nuclear concessions. Meanwhile, Iran's

leverage over the Strait of Hormuz has been weakening. According to
shipping data, for roughly half of September, Gulf oil exporters shipped
more crude than they were shipping before the war.

### Page 4

II. US Economy Continues To Roar
The US economy remains strong despite the volatility of the domestic
political and geopolitical scenes. Consider the following:
(1) Construction spending. Construction spending fell 1.9% m/m in
September to $2.19 trillion (saar), reversing most of August's upwardly
revised 2.0% increase. Nevertheless, spending remains just below last
year's record high (chart).
September's decline was driven by a 5.5% m/m drop in private residential

construction, attributable to a 13.0% plunge in home improvements
(chart). Elevated materials costs and higher mortgage rates continue to
weigh on the housing sector. Meanwhile, private nonresidential
construction rose 1.1% m/m, supported by continued investment in data
centers and power infrastructure.

### Page 5

The AI investment boom remains a powerful tailwind. Data center
construction spending surged 8.6% m/m and 90% y/y in September,
reaching a record $96.5 billion annual rate (chart).
(2) Manufacturing. The ISM M-PMI rose from 54.5 in September to 55.5
in October, marking its tenth consecutive month of expansion, the longest
streak since 2022. Production jumped to the highest reading since
November 2021, while new orders edged up to 55.5. Employment also

remained in expansionary territory (chart).

### Page 6

Inflationary pressures remain troublesome. The prices-paid index eased
from 77.9 to 75.2 in October but remained above 70 for the ninth
consecutive month. Regional Fed surveys also point to persistent price
pressures. Higher energy costs, tariffs, and elevated metals prices
continue to boost input costs (chart).
(3) Bank lending. The Fed's latest Senior Loan Officer Opinion Survey
(SLOOS) painted a mixed picture of credit demand. Banks reported
weaker demand for commercial and industrial (C&I) loans, mortgages,

and auto loans in Q4, while credit-card demand strengthened (chart).

### Page 7

Lending standards remained broadly unchanged for C&I loans but
tightened somewhat for commercial real estate loans (chart).
Encouragingly, actual bank lending tells a happier story. C&I loans surged
11.3% y/y through October 21, while total loans and leases increased
7.9% y/y (chart).

### Page 8

Credit growth is broad across bank sizes, with lending up 8.1% y/y at
large banks and 5.4% at small banks (chart).
Meanwhile, banks' allowances for loan and lease losses have remained
relatively stable at around $202 billion, despite the acceleration in lending
(chart).

### Page 9

clause.

Document 387: NZ labour market review chartpack, Q3 2026 (2026-11-06)

Source PDF: all report pdf/NZ labour market review chartpack, Q3 2026 (2026-11-06).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-11-06
Pages: 14
Status: ok

### Page 1

LABOUR MARKET CHARTPACK
4 Nov 2026

### Page 2

Westpac New Zealand Economics
2
Michael Gordon, Senior Economist | +64 9 336 5670 | michael.gordon@westpac.co.nz      Connect with us
Key points – September quarter 2026
• The September quarter surveys pointed to ongoing softness in labour market conditions. The unemployment rate rose from 5.6% to 5.7%, continuing an
almost five-year run of increases. The unemployment rate is now at its highest level since September 2015.
• There were mixed messages in terms of jobs growth: the Household Labour Force Survey reported a 0.1% fall in employment, while the employer-

focused Quarterly Employment Survey suggested a modest rise. The Monthly Employment Indicator had also pointed to a small rise in jobs.
• The fall in HLFS employment was accompanied by a fall in the participation rate. These co-movements essentially reversed the surprisingly large
increases that we saw in the June quarter, which we had suggested at the time were more likely due to sampling error in the survey rather than a
genuine surge.
• We generally recommend focusing on the unemployment rate, which is less vulnerable to sampling errors – and the message here is one of softness. Our

best interpretation is that, to the extent that jobs are growing, it’s not fast enough to absorb the growth in the population.
• While the economy has returned to growth over the last year or so, hiring activity remains weak. This may be an ongoing legacy of employment trends in
previous years, when firms were relatively reluctant to lay people off during the slowdown, which then potentially left them overstaffed even as activity
started to pick up again. It’s not clear how long this effect can persist, though.

• Wage growth remains muted. Although cost-of-living pressures have increased on workers, the existing degree of spare capacity in the labour market
doesn’t give them much leverage to negotiate larger pay increases.
• Overall, we judge these results to be weaker than the RBNZ expected, in particular the rise in the unemployment rate. This data supports the views of
those on the policy committee who were less convinced in October about the potential for second-round inflation pressures in the economy.

### Page 3

Westpac New Zealand Economics
3
Employment
• The number of people employed fell by 0.1% in the September quarter, according to the
Household Labour Force Survey (HLFS). Previous quarters were revised down a little,
reflecting downward revisions to the working-age population estimates. Annual employment
growth slowed from 0.9% to 0.7%.
• This was substantially softer than our forecast of a 0.5% rise for the quarter. Our forecast
was based on the Monthly Employment Indicator (MEI), a comprehensive measure that is

drawn from income tax data. While there are some differences in coverage for these two
measures, any significant divergence between the two is most likely to reflect sampling error
in the HLFS.
• Meanwhile, the employer-focused Quarterly Employment Survey (QES) recorded a 0.1% rise
in the number of filled jobs and a 0.6% rise in hours paid. (This survey is of course vulnerable
to sampling error as well.)
• We’re most inclined towards the MEI measure of employment, given the breadth of its

coverage. But our best interpretation is that, to the extent that jobs are growing, it’s not
yet fast enough to absorb the growth in the adult population – hence the further rise in the
unemployment rate.

### Page 4

Westpac New Zealand Economics
4
Labour force participation
• The surprising fall in HLFS employment was accompanied by a drop in the participation rate
from 70.6% to 70.4%.
• These co-movements are effectively a reversal of the surprisingly large increases that we
saw in the June quarter, which we noted at the time were suggestive of sampling error in the
survey. Any such errors largely wash out when it comes to the unemployment rate, however,
which is why we recommend focusing on this measure.

• Setting aside the usual quarterly volatility, labour force participation remains caught
between two competing forces. One is cyclical weakness, especially among younger people,
who are finding it difficult to break into a “low-fire, low-hire” labour market. The teen
unemployment rate of around 25% is a record high going back to the start of the survey
in 1986.
• The other force is rising participation among the over-60s (who also make up a growing

share of the population). Kiwis have had a growing propensity to work beyond the NZ Super
age, as there is no penalty for doing so. It’s unclear to what extent this can be considered
an increase in the pool of available workers: older workers tend to have low mobility
between jobs, and if they find themselves out of work are more likely to leave the labour
force altogether.

### Page 5

Westpac New Zealand Economics
5
Unemployment and underutilisation
• The unemployment rate rose from 5.6% to 5.7% in the September quarter. This was the
highest rate since September 2015.
• While there are other indicators of spare capacity in the labour market, we’re not convinced
that there is additional information to draw from them this time.
• The broader underutilisation measure, which includes discouraged jobseekers and part-
time workers wanting more hours, was steady at 13.8% after having risen sharply from

12.9% last quarter. This measure is both higher (by definition) and more variable than the
unemployment rate, but tells a similar story in that it’s around an 11-year high.
• There was a marked 1.6% fall in the number of hours worked (although ‘usual’ hours were
down just 0.4%). However, we suspect this was largely driven by Matariki – a floating holiday
that won’t be captured in the seasonal adjustment.
• This may also account for the 2.5% rise in part-time employment, against a 0.7% fall in

full-time employment. Some workers may have been pushed into the part-time category
(less than 30 hours a week) simply by virtue of only working four days during the
survey week.

### Page 6

Westpac New Zealand Economics
6
Hiring
• Business confidence surveys have shown a lift in hiring intentions in recent months, as firms
have shaken off the initial shock of the Iran conflict and the surge in fuel prices.
• However, those stated intentions aren’t obviously turning into reality yet. Job advertisements
were down about 1% for the September quarter in seasonally adjusted terms, and they
remain well below pre-Covid levels. Job ads will capture ‘churn’ within existing roles as well

as new job creation, but both of these would be associated with a strong labour market.
• With the working-age population growing at the same time, the result was a modest fall in
the vacancy rate, one of the RBNZ’s highest-ranked measures of labour market tightness.
• We estimate that there are around 1.9 people unemployed for every job vacancy, up from
around 1.8 people this time last year.
• Firms have told us that they were reluctant to lay off workers during the economic downturn

in 2023-24, as they were wary of the extreme labour shortages that had developed in the
previous boom years. As a result, they may find themselves still overstaffed even as demand
has started to pick up again, and haven’t needed to return to hiring yet.

### Page 7

Westpac New Zealand Economics
7
Population and migration
• The working-age population grew by around 1.0% over the last year, compared to a peak of
3.3% growth over 2023. Jobs growth (based on the less volatile MEI measure) is still lagging
behind population growth on an annual basis.
• The pool of potential workers is generally becoming older, with the Baby Boomers
increasingly reaching pension age and beyond, and with migrant arrivals tending to be an

older cohort than we saw pre-Covid – more in their 30s and 40s than their 20s.
• Net migration flows have started to pick up from their lows since late 2025. Departures of
New Zealanders have come down from their highs, though they remain above pre-Covid
levels, as the relative appeal of the Australian jobs market has narrowed.
• Foreign arrivals have picked up to some degree, although the growth appears to have been
mostly driven by students rather than those on work visas.

Michael Gordon, Senior Economist | +64 9 336 5670 | michael.gordon@westpac.co.nz      Connect with us

### Page 8

Westpac New Zealand Economics
8
Sectoral and regional trends
• Jobs growth has been relatively strong in government-connected sectors such as healthcare
and education, as well as in local and central government administration.
• Mining and utilities have seen strong growth in percentage terms, but neither of these
sectors are big employers in absolute terms.
• The construction sector is now starting to see a lift in employment, after a steep decline

over 2024-25. Residential building consents have risen strongly over the last year or so,
pointing to a growing pipeline of work for the homebuilding industry.
• Sectors that are connected to international tourism, such as transport and recreational
services, had previously seen strong employment growth, but this appears to have flattened
out in recent months.
• Employment trends have generally been more positive in the South Island than the North,

which likely reflects the greater influence of the agricultural sector – while not a large
employer in itself, farmers have benefited from high export prices and their spending has
flowed into other sectors.

Michael Gordon, Senior Economist | +64 9 336 5670 | michael.gordon@westpac.co.nz      Connect with us

### Page 9

Westpac New Zealand Economics
9
Wages
• The Labour Cost Index (LCI) rose by 0.5% in the September quarter. Annual growth
remained around the lowest since 2021.
• While the overall result was in line with our forecast, the details were a little weaker than
we expected, with less contribution from private sector wages (up 0.5%) and a surprisingly
large 0.7% increase in the public sector.
• The distribution of pay rates has returned to pre-Covid trends. Only 44% of roles saw a

pay rise over the last year; of those roles that did see an increase, the average size has
converged on the 2-3% range.
• The LCI is not a measure of what workers actually receive – it reflects the cost to businesses
for a given unit of labour – and hence shouldn’t be compared to the inflation rate. A more
suitable measure is the analytical unadjusted LCI, which includes pay rises that are related
to productivity gains. This measure slowed from 3.1% to 2.9% on an annual basis, the lowest

since March 2021.
• There was a marked pickup in the proportion of firms reporting cost-of-living adjustments as
the main reason for pay increases. However, the size of those increases varied greatly, with
many of them below the rate of CPI inflation.
• This seems to reflect a shift in the justification for pay rises, rather than an overall increase in
wage pressures – firms are more likely to cite cost-of-living adjustments because they’re less
likely to cite demand-side pressures.

### Page 10

Westpac New Zealand Economics
10
International comparisons
• New Zealand’s unemployment rate is the 18th lowest in the OECD (out of 38 countries). This
ranking has been broadly unchanged over the last year or so.
• Notably, New Zealand’s unemployment rate is substantially above Australia’s, whereas
historically it has been lower on average. This shift in relative job opportunities has been a
key attractor for New Zealanders over the last couple of years – as it was in the early 2010s

when the Australian economy was benefiting from a boom in mining construction.
• However, that gap between unemployment rates is no longer widening, with the Australian
jobs market starting to soften this year as the RBA has raised interest rates to rein in inflation
(which was already running too high before the Iran conflict and the surge in oil prices). We
expect Australia’s unemployment to rise further over the year ahead.
• New Zealand has the 7th highest employment rate in the OECD (down from 6th last quarter).

We tend to have high rates of labour force participation for 15-64 year olds, and particularly
high participation for the over-65s, as the lack of means testing for NZ Superannuation
means there is no financial disincentive to working beyond pension age.

• The global landscape remains complex, shaped by AI, 
energy prices and tariffs. 
• Taiwan’s AI cycle remains strong, with robust AI 
exports and investment and an intact semiconductor 
competitive moat. 
• AI is generating broader domestic spillovers, with 
wealth effects and wage growth supporting 
consumption. 
• Taiwan is cushioning the energy shock by diversifying 
energy imports and absorbing much of the increase in 
upstream costs. 
• AI-driven growth is likely to moderate from 4Q26 into

### Page 2

Taiwan: AI, energy and 4Q outlook October 13, 2026 
                                                                                                                                            2

Global backdrop

The global landscape remains complex, 
shaped by the interplay of AI, energy 
prices and tariffs, with significant 
implications for growth, inflation and 
financial conditions.

1) AI

The debate over the durability of the AI 
investment boom continues.  Tailwinds 
include technological advances, broader 
adoption, and s trategic and geopolitical 
considerations. Headwinds range from 
uncertainty over investment returns  and 
hyperscaler financing pressure to 
physical bottlenecks , safety concerns, 
and regulatory uncertainty.

Hyperscaler financing pressure has 
attracted increasing attention . In the 
early stages of the AI investment boom, 
hyperscaler capex was largely financed 
by internal cash flow. This is now 
changing, as free cash flow is expected 
to turn negative from 4Q, requiring major 
players to rely on debt issuance and 
other external financing increasingly.

At the same time, higher global interest 
rates are raising the cost of AI 
investment. The Fed resumed rate hikes 
at its September meeting, while global 
bond yields remain under upward 
pressure amid persistent inflation and 
elevated public and private borro wing 
needs. Our baseline assumes US rates 
will rise by 50bps this year and a further 
25bps in 2027 to 4.50%, with global 
long-term rates also remaining under 
pressure. This could increasingly weigh 
on the economics and pace of AI 
investment.

US-China AI competition is also drawing 
increasing attention.  Across the five 
layers of the AI ecosystem, China is 
making rapid progress in energy, 
infrastructure, AI models and 
applications, supported by its large scale, 
cost-effective domestic manufacturing 
supply chains and huge consumer 
market, which provides a wide range of 
real-world scenarios for AI adoption. The 
emergence of a more integrated and 
cost-competitive Chinese AI ecosystem 
could increasingly challe nge the US 
ecosystem, which currently operates at a 
relatively higher cost.

China, however, remains relatively weak 
in semiconductors.  Around half of 
China's domestic AI chip market still 
relies on foreign suppliers, including 
lower-end NVIDIA chips. This 
semiconductor bottleneck remains a key 
constraint on China's AI development. In 
our view, Taiwanese semiconductor 
companies' competiti ve moat therefore 
remains largely intact in the near term.

in 2027.

2) Energy

The US -Iran conflict remains at a 
standoff, keeping the energy -price 
outlook highly uncertain.  New 
developments are likely after the US 
midterm elections in November, with 
both further escalation and de -
escalation possible. Crude oil prices have 
remained elevated at around 
USD100/bbl amid a persistent 
geopolitical risk premium . In contrast, 
refined product prices have risen more 
sharply as refining disruptions and 
tighter product logistics have amplified 
the supply shock. Natural gas prices 
have also increased sharply, reflecting 
disruptions to LNG supply and limited 
alternative shipping routes.

Taiwan has been actively diversifying 
its energy import sources to cushion the 
impact. LNG imports from Qatar have 
fallen to zero since April, while imports 
from the US and other suppliers have 
surged to offset the shortfall, helping 
maintain stable domestic electricity 
supply.

                                                                                                                                            5

3) Tariffs

US tariff actions are likely to accelerate 
after the US midterm elections in 
November. Two major tariff measures 
remain in the pipeline. The Section 301 
forced-labor tariff was implemented in 
July, while final tariff action under the 
Section 301 overcapacity investigation, 
which covers 16 economies, remains 
pending. Meanwhile, Phase 1 of the 
Section 232 semiconductor tariffs was 
implemented in January, while Phase 2, 
covering a broader range of 
semiconductor-related products, 
remains pending.

Taiwan has secured a relatively 
favourable framework through its trade 
and investment agreements with the 
US. Taiwan signed an Agreement on 
Reciprocal Trade (ART) and an 
Investment MOU with the US earlier this 
year. The ART establishes a 15% US tariff 
rate on Taiwanese goods, while the MOU 
provides Taiwan with preferential 
treatment under Section 232, including 
for semiconductors. The ART has yet to 
enter into force, and it remains to be 
seen how the final Section 301 tariff 
burden will interact with the 15% ART 
framework. Meanwhile, major Taiwanese 
companies such as TSMC are expanding 
their US investments under the 
Investment MOU, which could support 
relatively favourable treatment for 
Taiwan when broader Section 232 
semiconductor tariffs are introduced.

Taiwan 4Q outlook

Taiwan has navigated the complex 
global environment relatively well so far 
this year, supported by robust AI exports 
and investment, diversified energy 
imports, and efforts to absorb higher 
upstream energy costs. Some 
normalisation is likely in 4Q26 –2027, as 
the AI -driven growth boost moderates 
and domestic energy prices face greater 
pressure to adjust.

1) Growth

GDP growth remains broadly on track 
with our expectations. High -frequency 
indicators suggest that GDP growth 
should remain around 12% yoy in 3Q, 
broadly in line with 12.9% in 2Q but below 
the 15.4% peak in 1Q. Growth is expected 
to moderate to a high -single-digit pace 
from 4Q onwards. We maintain our full -
year GDP growth forecasts at 11.6% for 
2026 and 5.6% for 2027.

The AI cycle remains the key driver of 
Taiwan's growth.  Information and 
communication products and electronic 
components accounted for nearly 80% 
of total exports in the first nine months 
of this year, while manufacturing 
accounts for nearly 50% of gross fixed

capital formation. From 4Q onwards, 
export growth is expected to moderate 
from exceptionally strong levels to a 
more normal but still robust pace, 
broadly following the trajectory of 
global hyperscaler capex and 
semiconductor sales. Investment is likely 
to follow a similar path.

For now, Taiwanese technology 
companies continue to report robust 
revenue growth, led by semiconductors, 
ODMs/EMS, and PCs and servers. 
Capacity utilisation at leading Taiwanese 
foundries has also continued to rise, 
suggesting that investment demand 
remains strong.

The AI boom is also beginning to 
generate broader spillovers to the 
domestic economy. The AI-driven equity 
rally has generated significant wealth 
effects, with the TAIEX up 70% year-to-
date and Taiwan becoming the world's 
fifth-largest stock market. Taiwanese 
households have benefited from the 
appreciation of financial assets, with 
equities and mutual funds accounting for 
nearly 30% of total household financial 
assets. The equity rally has also 
generated positive income effects. Wage 
growth has picked up, led by the services 
sector, particularly financial services, as 
strong earnings at financial 
institutions—especially securities 
firms—have prompted higher 
compensation to retain talent. These 
wealth and income gains should support 
household consumption, creating a 
positive feedback loop that broadens the 
benefits of the AI boom across the wider 
economy.

2) Inflation

CPI inflation is also tracking broadly in 
line with our forecast. Headline CPI 
remained elevated at 2.4% yoy in 3Q, up 
from 1.2% in 1Q and 2.2% in 2Q. We 
maintain our forecast for headline CPI 
inflation to remain close to 2.5% in 4Q, 
and retain our full-year forecasts at 2.0% 
for 2026 and 2.1% for 2027.

Energy inflation remains the main 
upside risk.  The government has 
absorbed much of the increase in energy 
costs through state -owned CPC and 
Taipower, limiting the pass -through to 
domestic consumers. With losses at both 
companies widening, the government 
will need to decide whether to provide 
additional subsidies to cover the losses 
or allow further increases in domestic 
energy prices. The September re view 
postponed the adjustment to the 
December meeting, when the overall 
situation will be reassessed after the 
November local elections. Taipower's  
latest review indicated that electricity 
rates would need to rise by 12.8%. A hike 
in electricity prices could lift inflation 
expectations and generate second -
round effects, particularly as the labour 
market tightens and wage pressures 
increase.

3) Monetary policy

The central bank kept the policy 
discount rate unchanged at 2.00% at its 
September meeting, in line with our 
expectations. We continue to expect a 
modest 12.5bp rate hike at the 
December meeting, bringing the policy 
rate to 2.125%.  By then, CPI inflation is 
likely to have remained above 2% for 
around six consecutive months. The 
possibility of an increase in electricity 
prices following the November local 
elections could further lift inflation 
expectations, strengthening the case for 
a December rate hike.

Financial conditions have already 
tightened despite the absence of a 
policy rate hike.  M2 growth remained 
around 7% yoy in 3Q, well below double-
digit nominal GDP growth. At the same 
time, bank loan growth has accelerated 
to more than 10% yoy, driven by strong

corporate credit demand, particularly AI-

companies. Tighter financial conditions 
are also evident in rising market interest 
rates, including commercial paper rates 
and government bond yields.

The central bank has continued to ease 
housing credit controls gradually. At its 
September meeting, it raised the LTV 
cap on second -home mortgage loans to 
70%, the second easing of housing credit 
controls this year. There is scope for 
further easing, including removing the 
LTV cap on second-home loans or raising 
the LTV caps for third - and fourth-home 
loans and high -value properties, which 
currently stand at 30%. However, the 
central bank is likely to proceed 
cautiously, given concerns that a faster 
relaxation could reignite  speculative 
demand and put renewed upward 
pressure on housing prices.

4) Housing

A gradual and modest recovery in the 
housing market is expected from 4Q26 
into 2027.  The existing -home market 
has already shown early signs of 
recovery, with Sinyi  housing prices 
returning to slightly positive yoy growth 
since 2Q. Total building transactions also 
returned to positive yoy growth in 3Q. 
The easing of credit controls should help 
reinforce this modest recovery trend.

The new -home market remains under 
pressure. The number of new units under 
construction has started to decline since 
2023, while new completions are  only 
beginning to ease this year . Demand 
remains weak, as potential buyers 
continue to wait for lower prices. Unsold 
new homes stood at 113K units at end -
2025, compared with a historical normal 
level of around 80K, implying excess 
inventory of roughly 30K units. At the 
current pace of new -home transactions, 
it would take roughly one year to 
normalize the existing excess inventory, 
and longer when factoring in the new 
supply pipeline. Some industry experts 
estimate that it could take as long as 3.8 
years digest current inventories fully 
(here).

Document 426: The FOMC pause to reflect (2026-11-06)

Source PDF: all report pdf/The FOMC pause to reflect (2026-11-06).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-11-06
Pages: 10
Status: ok

### Page 1

#fomc #usd #us
The FOMC pause to reflect
By Elliot Clarke
Head of International Economics, Westpac
08:14 October 29 2026 Share
No change in October, though confidence in the economy's strength and two dissents point
to a hike in December.
The FOMC held at their October meeting, albeit with two dissents, Governor Cook and Cleveland Fed
President Hammack preferring to hike by 25bps at this meeting. The statement was short and, in the
press conference, Chair Warsh again declined to provide definitive guidance on the near-term policy

### Page 2

Related articles
outlook.
The Committee remains positive on economic activity, characterising it in the statement as “expanding
at a strong pace” (an upgrade from “solid” in September). Despite elevated uncertainty, “domestic
spending has been resilient”, underpinned by “strong” productivity growth and “robust” capital
investment. Chair Warsh subsequently also emphasised the breadth of economic growth outside of
tech-related investment. Labour demand and supply were represented as balanced and resilient in

both the statement and press conference.
The Committee refrained from spotlighting key price risks in the statement. However, inflation “remains
elevated” and the FOMC are determined to achieve a “timely” and sustainable return of annual
consumer inflation to 2.0%yr after 6 years above target.
Along with the two dissents, this guidance and the “accumulation of more information will help the
Committee determine the policy stance” suggests another 25bp hike is likely in December. Thereafter,

the FOMC will remain attentive to any broadening in inflation and related risks through 2027.
Towards the end of the press conference, Chair Warsh also announced a structural change in the
FOMC schedule. While there will still be 8 meetings in 2027, a meeting in the fall will focus on
monetary policy strategy versus the current stance of monetary policy. Chair Warsh then intends for 2
of the 8 meetings in 2028 to have this strategy focus. If realised, this will see the FOMC review and

vote on the current stance of monetary policy just 6 times per calendar year from 2028.

Transitioning to mid-cycle 
• We now favour a more balanced stance between stocks and bonds . Near-
term event risks and high  earnings expectations prompt us to trim  equities to a 
Core holding.  The US and Asia ex -Japan (AxJ) remain our preferred regions. 
Within AxJ, we upgrade India to Overweight. 
• Higher bond yields are improving the outlook for future returns and offering 
increasingly attractive  income opportunities. We are Overweight Emerging 
Market (EM) USD bonds and favour corporate over government bonds, but limit 
duration to 3-7 years. 
• We remain  Overweight gold . Prices are likely to grind higher as near -term 
headwinds from higher bond yields are offset by EM central bank demand. 
Great expectations 
Individual risk events, high  earnings expectations and a higher cost of capital 
point to a more moderate return environment ahead.  In the very near term, 
financial markets must navigate key event risks, such as (i) the US mid-term elections, 
which have historically been associated with elevated market volatility and (ii) renewed 
geopolitical tensions, with several reports suggesting the US is considering renewed 
strikes on Iran . These  concerns have pushed the Brent oil price well above USD 
110/bbl despite significant ongoing crude oil flows through the Strait of Hormuz.  
Higher bond yields combined with high earnings expectations raise the risk of 
an expectations reset. The US Q3 earnings season is attracting significant attention 
given high earnings expectations, particularly f or the AI sector. However, higher US 
bond yields may start to bite as debt-financed capex takes on a greater share of total 
capex over the coming year. This, combined with already-high expectations, raises the 
risk of disappointment and a near-term repricing of expected earnings growth pace.  
Despite t he current c hallenges, healthy growth continues to underpin the 
economic backdrop . The US economic outlook remains well supported , and the 
absolute level of year-ahead earnings expectations is high. We do not expect Fed rate 
hikes to result in overly restrictive policy. Our caution, thus, stems from the risk of an 
expectations repricing rather than an outright economic slowdown.

1 Investment strategy and key themes

Steve Brice 
Global Chief Investment Officer 
Manpreet Gill

Raymond Cheng, CFA, CPA

Fig. 1 Our quant model score has fallen to levels

A more balanced stock-bond mix 
We trim global equities to a Core holding, leaving us 
Neutral on both equities and bonds and Overweight gold. 
The concerns laid out earlier point to near-term volatility risks 
and a somewhat lower long -term growth trajectory for 
equities. Our quant model also reflects  this cautious outlook, 
having downgraded equities to a level usually associated with 
flat-to-modestly-negative returns. 
From a relative perspective, though, the higher yields on 
offer from bonds also raise the bar  by presenting an 
increasingly compelling return outlook after several 
years of very low yields. We believe this argues for portfolios 
that are increasingly balanced between equities and bonds. 
Ways to diversify equity exposure 
We continue to favour global equity market 
diversification, with a preference for US and AxJ equities. 
We remain Overweight US and A xJ equities, expecting them 
to outperform other major regions. Earnings growth remains 
the main driver of this view – while growth rates could 
decelerate somewhat, we still expect the m to remain higher 
than those of other major regions.  
Japan and Euro area equities remain attractive as 
regional diversifiers.  Our positive view on both markets’  
financial sectors supports our Core holding view on them.   
Within Asia, we are Overweight Taiwan and India.  Indian 
equities have lagged the region this year, in part due to limited 
listed AI -related exposure. However, we believe this is 
increasingly likely to become an advantage as investors seek 
greater diversification away from excessive concentration in 
the AI theme. This comes at a time when the economic and 
earnings growth outlook for Indian equities is starting to 
improve. We retain a more Neutral view on Chinese equities 
amid a still-challenging growth outlook. 
We also favour greater sector diversification . In the US, 
we favour diversifying across our Overweight sectors of 
technology, financials, materials and healthcare. We also 
favour financials in Japan and the Euro area.  
Changes to our equity opportunistic idea s also reflect a 
more defensive stance. In recent weeks, we have closed our 
global semiconductors, MSCI Taiwan, US communication 
services and global power & electrification ideas and have 
opened new ideas on US healthcare and MSCI Switzerland. 
Riding the yield train 
We believe bond markets offer an attractive opportunity 
to lock in yields. The 10-year US government bond yield is 
now in a range last witnessed in 2007. While the process of 
rising yields results in lower bond prices – as painfully 
experienced in recent months  – the yield level itself starts to 
significantly improve the outlook for future returns. 
Favour corporate over government bonds, but check 
your duration. Still-high credit quality means that we continue 
to favour taking on credit risk to earn a higher yield (EM USD 
bonds are preferred, but we also see investment -grade (IG) 
and high -yield (HY) bonds as Core holdings). However, 
limiting duration to 3 -7 years remains key to avoiding 
excessive exposure to any inflation surprises. 
Changes to our bond opportunistic ideas also reflect a 
more balanced stance.  We close our EU  bank contingent 
convertible bonds (CoCos2 – please refer page 29 ) and US 
utilities corporate hybrids ideas. We also close our 
opportunistic idea on US 10-year government bonds following 
the recent pullback in yields, locking in gains. 
Alternatives and gold to remain relevant 
Alternatives are an effective  tool for portfolio risk 
management. Hedge funds and private markets provide a 
differentiated return stream , while  rotating into alternatives  
helps reduce  equity beta exposure . We examine downside 
capture and how beta is reduced via hedge fund sub -
strategies and private markets in our asset allocation section.  
Gold remains a key Overweight. We trim our three- and 12-
month forecasts modestly, but see gold forming a strong base 
above USD 4 ,000/oz. Rising bond yields and the USD were 
recent headwinds, but we expect a softer USD and continued 
EM central bank demand to result in 12-month gains.

The Foundation and Foundation+ models are allocations that you can use as the starting point for building a diversified 
investment portfolio. The Foundation model showcases a set of allocations focusing on traditional asset classes that are 
accessible to most investors, while the Found ation+ model includes allocations to private assets that may be accessible to 
investors in some jurisdictions, but not others.
